<meta>This page continues the scholarly presentation of the Nestorian Stele, a famous 8th-century monument discovered in China that records the arrival of Christianity. This section provides a Latin-to-English translation of the monument's theological narrative.</meta>

# INTERPRETATION III.

### Or

## PARAPHRASTIC DECLARATION

->Of the Chinese Inscription, first translated from Chinese into Portuguese, from that into<-
->Italian, and finally from Italian into the Latin language word for<-
->word, as follows.<-

---

<margin>O.</margin>
->The declaration of the *Xiù Più* <note>original: "Xiù Più"; likely referring to the title or preface of the inscription</note>, or as the Commentator says, made by a Priest<-
->of the Kingdom of Judea, who was called *Kim Lim* .<-

<margin>Creation of all things.</margin>
I. I say therefore in this manner: He who was always true and quiet, lacking any beginning, of the deepest and ever-enduring intellect, by His excellent power created all things from nothing; by His infinite majesty and holiness He made the Saints. This is the Divine essence, three in persons, and one in substance, our Lord, truly without beginning, *Olò*, or *yu* (which in Chaldean <note>The author uses "Chaldean" to refer to Syriac, the liturgical language of the Nestorian Church</note> signifies the same as *Eloha* <note>God</note>). In the figure of a Cross He made the four parts of the World; He moved the Chaos; <margin>Kis, what it means in the Chinese language.</margin> He made two *Kis* (that is, two virtues, or two qualities called *Inyam* <note>Yin and Yang</note>; the Commentator holds these to be two principles); He made a change in the abyss, that is, He changed the darkness; heaven and earth appeared; He made it so that the Sun and Moon by their motions caused night and day; He fabricated all things. Truly, in creating the first man, He furthermore bestowed upon him original justice, appointing him master of the entire Universe, who by his nature was at first empty and humble, full of himself, with a plain and even intellect, and without any mixture, having no inordinate appetite.

<margin>The Fall of Adam.</margin>
II. But after Satan used his frauds, he brought it about that Adam should infect that which was pure and perfect in himself and his nature; that is, he caused malice to begin to enter into him to disturb his peace, and by that fraud he introduced discord into the equality of this simplicity. <margin>365 sects.</margin> Therefore, three hundred and sixty-five sects having arisen one after another, each of them drew to itself the greatest number it could. Some held the creature in place of the Creator. Others posited the principle of all things as a vacuum, and a real being (the sect of the Pagodas <note>Buddhists</note> and the Chinese Literati <note>Confucian scholars</note> alludes to this), since they assert that the principle from which all things proceeded is a vacuum; which to them is the same as subtle, imperceptible to the senses, even if in itself it is a real and positive principle. But the Literati say that the principle of things is not only real and positive, but furthermore that it is of such figure and corpulence that it can be comprehended by the senses. Some sought blessedness through sacrifices. Some boasted of a certain goodness to deceive men, in which matter they placed all their science and industry, serving their own affections with all diligence and intention. But they labored in vain and without profit, always progressing toward the worse, just as happens to those who wish to draw fire from a clay vessel, adding darkness to darkness, and by this very act losing the true path, not knowing how to return to the way of life.

<margin>Incarnation of the Son of God.</margin>
III. Then one of the Divine persons of the most holy Trinity, called the *Messias* <note>Messiah</note>, by restraining and covering His Majesty, and accommodating Himself to human nature, was made man. For which reason

<sig>D 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: to</meta>

<meta>This page continues the description of the arrival of Christianity in China, specifically detailing the life of Christ, the practices of his ministers, and the official reception of the missionary Alopen by the Emperor.</meta>

# OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

...to announce this joy, He sent an Angel, and He was born of a Virgin in Judea. A great star was the announcer of this happiness. Kings, having seen its brightness, came to offer gifts, so that the **Law** and the prophecies of the twenty-four Prophets might be fulfilled. Moreover, He governed the world with a great Law; He established a Divine **Law**, spiritual and without the noise of words, and perfected it through true faith. He arranged the eight beatitudes; He changed worldly things into eternal ones; He opened the gate of the three virtues (the Theological virtues <note>Faith, Hope, and Charity</note>); He gave life by destroying death. In His own person, He descended to the lower regions and threw all demons into confusion; in the ship of His piety, He led the good to heaven and vindicated the souls of the righteous unto salvation. Finally, these things being finished, by His own power He ascended into heaven around midday, leaving behind twenty-seven volumes of Doctrine to open the gate for the great conversion of the world. He instituted Baptism by water and the spirit to wash away sins and to return the world to purity. He uses the Cross so that He may embrace all without exception; He rouses everyone with the voice of charity, commanding them to show reverence toward the East so that they may proceed on the way of a glorious life.

<margin>
The life of the Apostles, Priests, or Ministers of Christ.
</margin>

IV. For the sake of outward ornament, His ministers grow beards and make crowns upon their heads (that is, they shave the crown of the head <note>The tonsure</note>) to show that they are held by no evil affection within. They do not use slaves; in high and low (that is, in prosperity and adversity) or in humble estate, they make themselves equal to all. They do not gather riches but make them common to all. They observe fasts both to mortify the passions and to keep the Divine precepts. They highly esteem men who are elevated above themselves and abstracted (from worldly affairs). Seven times a day they offer prayers for both the living and the dead; once every seven days they perform sacred rites to purge the soul from sins and restore its purity. Because the true and constant Law is excellent,

<margin>
The excellence of the Christian Law.
</margin>

it is difficult to find a suitable name for it, since its effect is to illuminate and drench all things in brightness; hence it was necessary to call it *Kim kiào*, that is, the Bright and Great Law.

V. Where Royal persons are lacking, the Law does not extend or spread; but Royal persons are by no means magnified without the Law. Therefore, when the Law and Kings are in harmony and unite as if into one thing, the world is immediately illuminated. Thus, when the King called *Taì cùum veù huam-ti* <note>Emperor Taizong of Tang</note> was administering (China) in that celebrated time with singular prudence and holiness, there came from Judea a man of highest virtue called *Olò puen* <note>Alopen</note>, who, guided by the clouds, brought the true Doctrine. Guided also by the winds and hydrographic charts, he endured many dangers and labors, and finally, in the year *Chin quon* and *ieù fiè* (that was the year of Christ 636), he arrived at the Royal Court.

<margin>
The Law is brought into China.
</margin>

The King indeed commanded a famous *Colào* <note>A high-ranking official or Chancellor</note> named *Fam Kieù lym* to take a beast of burden and go to meet the new guest toward the Western part—that is, toward the suburb of the City—and, having received him with all benevolence, to introduce him into the Palace. The King ordered the Doctrine to be translated in the Palace, and there he investigated the truth of the Law together. The King understood it to be the true Law and seriously ordered it to be published and spread throughout the whole Kingdom with efficacy and honor; and in this 12th year of *Ciu quon*, in the 7th month of Autumn (that was the year of the Lord 636), he wrote back in this manner. (The tenor of the proclamation is as follows:) The true Law does not have a fixed name, nor do the saints have a fixed place where they remain; they run out to all parts to teach the world, intent on succoring the laboring world with sails and oars to bring utility.

<margin>
It is spread through China.
</margin>

From this Kingdom of *Tancìn* (or Judea), *Olò puen*, a man of great virtue, brought the Doctrine and images from such distant places to be presented to our Royal Court.

<margin>
The veneration of Holy Images.
</margin>

<summary>This page describes the core tenets of the "Bright Law" (Christianity) as understood in 17th-century missions, detailing the arrival of the missionary Alopen at the court of Emperor Taizong in 636 AD.</summary>

CHINA ILLUSTRATED.

Examining the intent of his teaching from its foundations, we have found his doctrine to be exceedingly excellent and without outward clamor, based principally upon the Creation of the World. His doctrine does not consist of many words, nor does it establish its truth merely on the surface, bringing salvation and progress to men: wherefore it is fitting that it be spread throughout our entire Empire.

<margin>He takes care that a Church be built.</margin>
He also commanded the Mandarins <note>High-ranking officials in the Chinese imperial bureaucracy.</note>, who are called *Nim fam*, in that place of the Court, to build a great Church and to appoint twenty-one Ministers to it. (The author *Kim Cim* here praises the King) namely for weakening the forces of the Monarchy of *Cheù Olad iù* (this is the head of the sect *Stai iù*, others call it *Taufu* <note>Likely a reference to Taoism.</note>). He departed in a black chariot toward the West (that is, outside of China). But when the great and illustrious *Tam* <note>The Tang Dynasty.</note> was established with the *Tao* <note>The Way or Principle.</note>, the holy Gospel was brought into China. Shortly thereafter, the King ordered the portrait of that man (*Olò puèn* <note>Alopen, the first Nestorian missionary to China.</note>) who, as we have said, first brought the Gospel, to be painted and hung upon the wall; his excellent likeness shone at the gates of the Church, and his memory will always shine in the world.

<margin>Portrait of Olopuen, the first Apostle of the Chinese.</margin>

<margin>Description of the location of Judea according to Chinese Geographers.</margin>
VI. According to the Geographers who mention the Western parts, and according to the Historians of the two Kingdoms of *Han* and *Guei*, the Kingdom of *Tan cin* (that is, Judea <note>original: "Tan cin (id est Judæa)"—Kircher identifies the Chinese term 'Da Qin' with Judea, though it often referred to the Roman Empire.</note>) is bordered on the South by the Red Sea, on the North it borders the Mountains of Gems, on the West the *Boco das sullas* (what this might be is difficult to guess; I suspect it is Mount Carmel) in view of the Holy Places. Finally, on the East it is bordered by this place *Ciam fam* and the water which they call dead <note>The Dead Sea.</note>. This land belches forth ashes heated by fire (perhaps he means the Asphalt Lake <note>The Dead Sea.</note>); it produces balsam, small gems, and carbuncles (by which he suggests Egypt along with the rest of the shore of the Red Sea, where the aforementioned things are found). It lacks robbers and assassins; the people live in peace and joy; they admit nothing into the Kingdom except the Gospel; dignities are conferred on none except those deserving by virtue: the buildings are grand: (in a word) the Kingdom is famous for poetry, order, and good manners.

VII. *Docào*, others call him *Caozum* <note>Emperor Gaozong of Tang.</note>, the son of *Taì çùm*, took up the reins of the Kingdom in the year of our Lord 651. (Continuing, the author *Kim cim* says these things): *Caò vìm*, others *Caozum*, a great Emperor not at all degenerate from the virtues of his Grandfather, knew how to continue with honor the intent of his Grandfather, and to enlarge and honor the affairs of his father, commanding Churches to be built in all the Provinces, and at the same time honoring *Olò puèn* with the title of Bishop of the Great Law, which governs the Kingdom of China.

<margin>The Gospel is preached in the ten Provinces of China.</margin>
Then the law of God was promulgated through the ten Provinces (of which the Chinese Kingdom was composed) and the Kingdom enjoyed the highest peace; all cities were filled with Churches, and houses flourished with the happiness of the Gospel.

<margin>Persecution is stirred up against the law.</margin>
VIII. In this year called *Xim liè* (which was then the year of our Lord 699), the followers of the Pagodas, the Bonzes <note>Buddhist monks.</note>, using their influence, raised their voices (that is, they blasphemed our holy Law) in the place called *Tum Cieù* (it was perhaps in the Province of *Honan*) and at the end of this other year called *Sien tien* (which corresponds to the year of our Lord 713), certain private men in *Sienò*, which was the ancient Royal city of *Veñ vam* (the Commentator wishes this to be *Siganfù* <note>Xi'an.</note>, in the Province of *Xensì*), dared to tear apart our holy Law with jests, mockery, and reproach.

IX. At this time there was a certain prince of Priests (he seems to have been a Bishop) named *John*, and another man of great virtue named *Kie Lie*. These two, with other noblemen of their people, no less famous for their reputation than for their contempt for worldly things, began again to duly explain their excellent Gospel by continuing it, and to repair the threads (broken by the malice of the Evil Demon) long ago. The King called *Hiuen cum chi tao* (his Empire began in the year 719) commanded five Princes that they should go in person to the happy house (that is, the Church) and erect altars. Then the column of the Law, which

<margin>The Law revives.</margin>

<page-num>32</page-num>

which had lain prostrate for a short time, began to rise again and take on new growth. In this beginning of the year *Tien pao* <note>The Tianbao era, beginning in 742 AD</note> (which was the year of our Lord 743), the King <note>The Emperor Xuanzong</note> *Ota Ciam Kuen* (a title of honor) commanded the aforementioned *Cuoliè fiè* (he was a Eunuch prevailing with the highest favor and authority before the King) to carry the true likenesses of the five Kings, his predecessors and ancestors, and place them in the Church, and at the same time to bring one hundred treasures of precious things to the Church to celebrate this solemnity. (The author *Kim Cim* says in praise of these Kings:) Even if the long beards of the Dragon are long, nevertheless his bows and swords can be grasped by hands. <margin>A Chinese Fable.</margin> (He alludes here to a certain ancient history of the same King) who was imagined to have ascended into the air sitting upon a Dragon, whom the servants—who said they would go with the King—had equipped with various kinds of weapons; but those who remained, plucking at the Dragon's beard, carried away certain weapons in memory of the King, gazing upon them as if he were present. (The Author of this writing therefore alludes to this history to declare that the likenesses of the mentioned Kings were in the place of those present and living to the King; therefore he adds: The brightness which these images give back shows that they are present here with us.)

<margin>In the year 745, another Preacher arrived from Judea.</margin>
X. In the third year of *Tien pao* (that was the year of our Lord 745), there was in Judea <note>original: "Judaea"; likely referring to the Roman East or Syria</note>—others say in India—a certain Priest *Kieho*, who, guided by the help of the stars, arrived in China, and looking toward the Sun (this was the ceremony of those who were admitted to conversation with the King), he was introduced to the sight of our Emperor. Moreover, the King commanded that the Priests John and Paul, with other men of the same profession, and with the present Man of such great virtue *Kie ho*, should betake themselves to the palace called *Him Kim* to worship and to exercise holy works of devotion. At this time, the Royal letters were preserved on the tablets of the Church, preciously adorned according to their order, shining with red and blue color; and the Royal pen filled the empty space, ascending and reaching even unto the Sun: his favor and gifts are compared to the height of the Southern mountain, and the abundance of his benefits equals the depth of the Eastern sea. Reason cannot but approve what is approved and worthy of remembrance. Therefore the King <margin>In the year 757, King Sa cum nen and his virtues.</margin> called *Sa Cum nen* or *Ven min* <note>Emperor Suzong</note> (who began to reign in the year 757) commanded Churches to be built by a new mandate in this *Lim suù* and in five other cities. This King was of extraordinary character, under whom the gate of happiness was opened to the whole Kingdom; with this happiness, joy, and applause existing, the matters of Royal government were greatly elevated.

<margin>In the year 764, the virtues of King Tai cum.</margin>
XI. King *Tai cum ven vu* <note>Emperor Daizong</note> (he entered into Empire in the year 764), having been made a sharer of good times, administered the business of the Kingdom without difficulty: On the Feast of the Nativity of Christ <note>Christmas</note> each year, he sent celestial perfumes as an act of thanksgiving, and for honoring the ministers of this holy Law, he assigned a provision of all things and of the Royal grain; Certainly heaven grants beauty and perfection to the World, and therefore liberally produces things; This King imitated heaven, and for that reason knew how to nourish and sustain his people.

<margin>In the year 781.</margin>
XII. King *Kien cum xim ven vu* <note>Emperor Dezong</note> (reigning in the year 781), used eight ways to reward the good and punish the wicked. He instituted new methods for restoring and promoting the state of the Gospel. His government was exceedingly excellent; we pray to God for him, not being ashamed in this. He was of great virtue, humble, peaceful, and learned; he tolerated his neighbor, abounded in charity to help all, and did good to all living beings. <margin>The effects of the Gospel and the Christian Law.</margin> This is the true way, and the ladder of our holy Law: to make it so that the rain and winds return at their accustomed times, the World be quiet, men well governed, individual things rightly disposed, and that the living may live well,

<meta>catchword: live well,</meta>

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

<page-num>33</page-num>

...that the living and the deceased might enjoy joy together. To have these things ready at hand and to give an account of them proceeds, in truth, from our holy faith, and all these effects are the strength and power of our holy Gospel.

<margin>Gifts of the King made to the preacher of the Word of GOD.</margin>
**XIII.** To the Priest named *Usù* <note>original: *Yisi*, a prominent Christian benefactor and military official</note>, a great proclaimer of the Law, the King <note>The Emperor</note> gave these titles: *Kin iù, quàm lò tai fù* (this is an office in the Royal court) and *Soù fùm ciè tù fù lèi* (an office outside the court) and *Xi tieñ thum Kieñ* (also another office, the explanation of which I did not find in the Manuscript). Likewise, he gave to the said Priest, the proclaimer of the great Law, an Ecclesiastical garment of a blue color (in Italian, *pavonazo* <note>a deep violet or peacock blue</note>). This Priest was peaceful; he rejoiced in doing good to others, performing works of virtue with the greatest zeal. He arrived in China from a remote region, from a place called *Vam xi ciù chìm* in the region of the Pagodas <note>original: *Wushe Qicheng*, likely referring to Rajagriha in India</note>, which is the same as the far-distant region of India. His achievements surpassed three famous generations of China, and he perfectly expanded the remaining sciences. In the beginning, he served the King in the court; later, his name was also entered into the Royal Book.

**XIV.** The Prince of *Fuen yam* <note>Fenyang</note>, also called *Cò çù y* <note>Guo Ziyi, the famous general who quelled the An Lushan Rebellion</note>, illustrious with the title *Chum Xulim*, initially applied himself to military affairs in the regions of *Sofam*. But the King called *Sò çùm* <note>Emperor Suzong</note> commanded *Ay sù*, or *Y sù*, to promote *Cò çù y* more favorably than the others (it seems the King ordered him to be the Counselor to *Cò çù y*). Even though he was greatly loved by the Captain, he did not care for his ordinary style of proceeding; for he was the "claws and teeth" of the Republic, the "eyes and ears" of the Army (a Chinese phrase). He knew how to distribute his income and not accumulate it at home. He offered to the Church a precious thing called *Poli* (it seems to have been made of glass) from this place *Cim regueñ*, others say *Lintiguen*. Furthermore, he offered carpets interwoven with gold from this place, called *Ciè Kì*. He repaired old Churches, founding them anew and establishing the courtyard and the house of the Law; adorning the houses and lodgings so they were shining like flying pheasants. Besides the exercises of our holy Law, he applied himself to <margin>The King's works of charity.</margin> works of charity, gathering the Priests of the four Churches every year, whom he served with his whole heart, providing all necessary things for the space of fifty days; he also fed the hungry, clothed the naked, healed the sick, and buried the dead.

**XV.** In the time of *Tà sò* <note>likely a reference to a Buddhist leader or a specific era</note>, with all his parsimony, such goodness was never heard of (this *Tà sò* was a Bonze <note>term for a Buddhist monk</note> of the Pagoda sect) who, in a certain very large Congregation of Bonzes in which the affairs of his sect were to be handled, performed the duty of receiving guests and procuring necessary things for everyone. (Therefore, the Author, in treating the works of charity, places *Oy fiè* <note>the Christian benefactor</note> before *Tan sò*.) But in the time of this Gospel, we see similar men devoting themselves to holy works. Therefore, so that such heroic works might come into the light, I wished to engrave them upon this great Stone. <margin>The happy state of the Chinese Church is carved into the Stone.</margin>

**XVI.** I say, therefore, that the true **GOD** is without beginning, pure, quiet, and immutable; He was the first artisan of Creation; He opened the earth and raised the heavens. One of the persons, for the eternal salvation of mortals, was made man; He ascended on high like the Sun, destroying darkness and establishing profound truth in all things.

**XVII.** That most splendid King, who was truly the first of the first Kings, using the opportunity, removed every difficulty; the heavens were expanded, and the earth extended. Most clear is our Gospel, which was brought into the Kingdom of *Tam* <note>The Tang Dynasty</note>, bringing doctrine, erecting Churches; it served as a ship for the living and the deceased, exalting all happiness and giving rest to the whole World.

<margin>Erection of Churches.</margin>
**XVIII.** *Caò çum* <note>Emperor Gaozong</note>, following in the footsteps of his Grandfather, again applied his mind to building new Churches. High and magnificent temples of peace filled all the land. The true Law put on beauty.

<meta>This page continues Athanasius Kircher’s Latin translation and commentary on the Nestorian Stele (the "Syro-Chinese Monument"), a 7th-century stone tablet discovered in China that records the arrival of Christianity during the Tang Dynasty.</meta>

# THE WORKS OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

<margin>The title of the Bishop and the esteem held for him.</margin>
He honored the Bishop with a title, as the people enjoyed tranquility and joy without hardships.

XIX. The wise King *Ni uen çum* <note>Emperor Suzong of Tang</note> knew how to walk the true and straight paths: the Royal tablets were magnificent and illustrious, and the Royal letters upon them flourished and shone. The Royal figures were radiant: All the people deeply venerated them, all things were expanded, and men enjoyed the highest joy from it.

XX. This King *Sò çum* <note>Emperor Daizong of Tang</note>, reigning in his own person, came to the Church; the holy Sun shone brightly: happy clouds dispelled all the darkness and obscurity of the night. With happiness gathered in the Royal house, evils ceased, and with dissensions removed, our Empire was restored.

<margin>The splendor of the Church.</margin>
XXI. This King *Tai çum fù* <note>Emperor Dezong of Tang</note> was obedient; in virtue, he equaled heaven and earth; he gave life to the people and progress to every individual thing. He sent incense to the Church in thanksgiving and practiced works of charity. The Sun and Moon were united in his person—that is, everyone flew to him to offer their obedience.

<margin>Illuminated by the articles of faith.</margin>
XXII. This King *Kieñ cum* <note>The era name for Emperor Dezong</note>, established in his governance, rendered virtue clear: by arms, he pacified the four seas; by letters, he enlightened ten thousand borders: like a candle, he illuminated the secrets of men, and as if in a mirror, he saw all things detected; he revived the whole world: all the Barbarians <note>A term used here for non-Chinese peoples or foreigners</note> received the rule of living.

<margin>The Divine Law.</margin>
XXIII. The Law—oh, how great, how perfect, and how it extends itself to all things! Wishing to name it, I could not help but call it the Divine Law. Kings knew how to arrange their affairs; I, a Vassal, am able to recount them; therefore, I erect this most precious Stone in proclamation of great happiness.

<margin>The Stone erected in the year 782.</margin>
XXIV. In our Empire of the great *Tam* <note>The Tang Dynasty</note>, in the second year of this *Kieñ cum* (namely the year of our Lord 782), on the seventh day of the Autumn month, on the Lord's Day <note>Sunday</note>, this Stone was erected, while the Bishop *Him ciù* was administering the Chinese Church; a Mandarin named *Liu siëci yen*, who held the title of *Ciao y cum*, and others *Chiò y lam*, in which office before him was *Tai cieù fiè su Can Kiun*, completed this inscription with his own hand.

And this is the explanation of the inscription carved into the rock, in which it is truly worthy of the highest admiration that such a varied and manifold account of things, which this stone contains, could be encompassed in so narrow a space and in no more than one thousand and eighteen characters. Moreover, a twofold interpretation of this writing was made; the present one is very much in conformity with that which was made at *Pequini* <note>Beijing</note>, and then also set forth again from the Chinese original by Father Michael Boym, which is both more elegant and more proper, and more conformed to the Chinese phrasing; and although for that same reason it may appear less elegant in our language, it is nonetheless most elegant, and regarding its style and Chinese phrasing, it is most highly praised by all those who profess to be masters of the said language. <margin>At what time the Christian Law was brought into China.</margin> Furthermore, from this Monument is gathered, first, the introduction of the Christian Law into the Kingdom of China, which occurred about a thousand years ago. Likewise, with how much honor and veneration it was received by the Emperors; how and through how many Provinces it was promulgated; how much it was expanded during the 150 years in which it flourished most; and finally, what kind of persecutions it twice endured; thus it was gradually suppressed by them, so that unless the memory of it had remained in this Monument, no traces of it would survive any longer. Certainly, in the Chinese Chronicles, concerning its introduction into China, beyond the names of the aforementioned Kings and Mandarins, scarcely anything is found. Moreover, this Stone was found a few years before the Fathers of the Society of JESUS arrived in China; from which it appears that the interpretation of such extraordinary and sublime matters...

...that the [revelation] was not reserved for anyone except those who, being excellently instructed in the knowledge of divine and human affairs, were destined to be the announcers and propagators of this same Doctrine and Law, which had been obliterated by the passage of time. And let these remarks suffice regarding the inscription made in Chinese characters. However, since many things in this translation are obscure, I have decided to illuminate them with Commentaries <note>original: "Scholiis"</note>, together with the consensus of the doctrine of all Orthodox Orientals which is contained therein.

## CHAPTER IV.
### *Concerning the Cross carved on the very top of the Stone.*

<margin>Changes in affairs are usually preceded by prodigies.</margin>
It is so arranged by Divine providence that when a significant change in the state of a Kingdom or Republic is imminent, it is accustomed to manifest that change, before it happens, through certain prodigies and monstrous omens. The records of all historians are full of the great prodigies seen in the Roman Empire shortly before the coming of Christ. Let him who wishes read Hegesippus, Josephus, and others regarding the great portents that preceded the destruction of Jerusalem. Just as these things are confirmed by the irrefragable testimony of writers in all revolutions of Republics, so too were they verified in Japan and the vast Empire of China—already ripe for conversion—by the new discovery of the most holy Cross. <margin>A Cross found in a split tree in Japan.</margin> In Japan, a Cross found by those splitting a tree drew everyone into admiration by the novelty of the event. We read in the *History of the West Indies* <note>A common 17th-century term for the Americas.</note> that there was a man in the Mexican Kingdom, a leader of sacrifices, whom they held as a Prophet; <margin>Prophecy concerning the discovery of America.</margin> he is said to have stated, shortly before the discovery of America or the New World, that a time was coming and was already near at hand when a foreign race arriving from the East would subjugate their entire Kingdom to its empire, and the statues of their idols would flee at the first sight of the Cross which they carried with them. This happened exactly in the manner he predicted; for Ferdinand Cortez had scarcely reached the shore of the unknown Kingdom when, behold, unfurling a black banner marked with a red Cross, and relying on its miraculous power together with a trust in God for the successful conduct of affairs, he took possession of the entire Kingdom from the subject gentiles. <margin>The Cross of the Kingdom of Chile.</margin> I will not speak here of the tree growing in the shape of a Cross shortly before the Christian faith was introduced into the Kingdom of Chile, as the records of the Indian Annals of our Society <note>The Jesuits.</note> are full of these things, just as was treated by me at length in *Our Little Work on the Prodigious Crosses seen on the clothes of men in the year 1660 at Naples*. But to return to the Chinese: Father Boym <note>Michał Boym, a Polish Jesuit and scientist.</note> hinted above in the Preface to the Reader that various signs of the Cross had been found in China. <margin>A huge iron Cross found in Honan.</margin> Near the *Chiang* river in the Province of Honan, at nearly the same time as this Monument, a huge iron Cross was found. <margin>Crabs marked with a cross never seen before.</margin> In some provinces, crabs were also found, never seen before this time, which were marked on their backs with the figure of a white Cross. Indeed, in the Province of Shaanxi <note>original: "Xiamſi"</note> itself, Father Matteo Ricci first learned from a certain Hebrew Chinese Mandarin that some men had been found whom they called "worshippers of the Cross." But since the matter is worthy of consideration, we will briefly touch upon it from the *History of Father Trigault*.

Now I gird myself all the more gladly to collect the remains of Christian truth and follow its tracks, because I know it will be most pleasing to our people in Europe. <margin>Father Nicolas Trigault on the worshippers of the Cross.</margin> We received word of this in previous years from the same Jew and other indications. When it already became clearly established to Father Matteo Ricci...

# ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

<page-num>36</page-num>

...that Chinese graduate <note>original: "licentiatum," referring to a person who has attained a specific academic degree</note> was a Professor of the Ancient Law <note>referring to Judaism</note>, he applied his mind to discovering some clearer trace of the Christian matter than what we had possessed until then. And indeed, as long as he called them by this name, he achieved nothing; but gradually describing various dogmas of the Law to them, when he introduced talk of the Cross—which he most desired—he succeeded. Among the Chinese there is no use of the Cross, and therefore no name for it; for which reason our men bestowed a Chinese name upon it, borrowing it from the character <margin>The Chinese denote the number ten with the figure of a Cross.</margin> which signifies the number ten, and expresses a perfect Cross by this sign: **+**. And perhaps it happened not without divine will that our men today should bestow the same name on the Cross which the ancients in China, compelled by the same poverty of language, had already bestowed long ago: for both called it *Scie-cu*, that is, the character for the number ten. Nor did they depart in this from the example of the Sacred Scriptures, by which the letter **T** <note>The Tau cross</note> is designated, also borrowing the figure of the Cross for its perfection. When, therefore, a conversation was introduced about the Cross called by this name, that Israelite related that in the capital Kaifeng, his homeland, and in another very celebrated marketplace named Linqing in the Province of Shandong, and in the Province of Shanxi, there lived certain foreigners whose ancestors had come from the Western Kingdoms. He said they were worshippers of the Cross, and were accustomed to trace it with a finger over their food and drink, but neither he nor they thought they knew why they practiced that rite. This testimony of the Israelite agreed with what the Fathers had already heard for a long time from various people regarding the same rite of tracing the Cross used in various places; indeed, that the same character of the saving sign was marked in ink on the foreheads of infants against childhood misfortunes. With these things also agrees what Girolamo Ruscelli <note>original: "Hieronymus Rusellus," a 16th-century Italian humanist</note> had noted in his commentaries on Ptolemy’s *Cosmography* when speaking of the Chinese. And since we are dealing with the Cross <margin>Other traces of the Christian faith.</margin> among the Chinese, another trace of it must by no means be omitted. One of our Fathers saw in the hands of an antiquarian a very elegant bell made of bell-bronze for sale, on the top of which a sacred shrine was engraved, and opposite the shrine a Cross, and in the circumference several Greek characters. He who saw it wished to purchase it for himself, but they could by no means agree on a price, nor did that antiquarian ever appear again afterward so that those characters could be transcribed. That same Israelite added that those very worshippers of the Cross took the part of the Doctrine which they recited in place of prayers from their own books, and that it was common to both <note>meaning both Jews and these Christians</note>; perhaps he understood the Davidic Psalter. He asserted that these people had been very numerous, especially in the Northern Provinces, and had flourished so much in letters and arms that they created suspicion among the Chinese, who are by nature very prone to fearing innovations. He judged that this suspicion was stirred up no more than sixty years ago by the Saracens <note>Muslims</note>, the bitterest enemies of the Christian name in the whole world. That suspicion reached such a point that, since they feared hands would be laid upon them by the magistrates, <margin>The worshippers of the Cross go into hiding.</margin> they all fled here and there; some professed themselves Saracens, others Jews, and most of them worshippers of idols out of fear of death. Their temples were changed into shrines for idols, and he even called the "Temple of the Cross" among his people by its own name, by which it is now called since it has been a shrine. And from that time they have been so dismayed by fear that they hide nothing more than that they are descended from that race. Whence it happened that when our brother betook himself there to explore the remnants of the Christians, and carried with him the names of the families mentioned by the Jew, there was no one among them who would profess himself as such—perhaps because our brother was recognized by his face to be Chinese, and they suspected perhaps that he was a spy sent by the magistrates. Nor has an European priest been able to be sent there until now due to the small number of our men, although it will be necessary to establish a seat there at leisure to remove various fears, which will one day be achieved, God willing. Thus says Trigault <note>Nicolas Trigault, a Jesuit missionary to China</note>.

Certainly in the time of Marco Polo the Venetian, in the year 1286, just as there were very many Christians in Tartary, so also the veneration and use of the Cross flourished greatly, as he himself testifies as an eyewitness <note>original: "αὐτόπτης" (autoptes)</note>. For when Nayan, <margin>The history of Nayan the Christian, from Marco Polo the Venetian.</margin> a Christian by religion, sought the Kingdom of Kublai, the Great Emperor of the Tartars, and had already prepared an army of four hundred thousand with the auxiliary forces of another King, Kaidu, Kublai...

<margin>The testimony of King Kublai concerning Christ and his cross.</margin>
Saracens, and Christians, turned to the latter and spoke thus: « *Our God and His Cross indeed did not wish to bring any protection to Naiam; but do not be ashamed on that account, because God, being good and just, ought by no means to patronize iniquity and injustice. Naiam stood forth as a traitor to his lord and stirred up rebellion against all equity, and indeed implored the help of your God in his malice; but He, as a good and righteous God, would not favor those crimes.* » Whereupon the Jews and Saracens, enemies of the Christian name, were forbidden from presuming to tear at the God of the Christians or His Cross with further blasphemies, and in this way silence was imposed upon them. These things are from Marco Polo the Venetian. From which it is clear that the Christian religion flourished greatly in those regions, about which more will be said later at length.

# CHAPTER V.

## Concerning the Articles of Faith and other ceremonies and rites contained in the Monument.

<margin>The Holy Trinity.</margin>
The Holy Trinity is contained in such express words in the Monument <note>Referring to the Xi'an Stele, an 8th-century stone documenting early Christianity in China</note> that I consider a more lengthy exposition of it to be quite unnecessary; for after it had diligently explained the essence and nature of God in the second point through appropriate attributes, it later adds: [*This Divine essence, triple in persons, and in one substance.*] which suffices to have noted. This triune substance is *Olo oy*, 
<margin>ܐܠܘܗܐ</margin>
which in the Chaldean <note>Syriac</note> <term>ܐܠܘܗܐ</term> *Eloho* <note>God</note> perhaps indicates. In the figure of the Cross, He made the parts of the four-fold world. He moved the chaos—this alludes to the primordial creation of things performed out of a chaotic mass. [*He made two Kis,*] that is, principles, which the Chinese commentator says are nothing other than matter and form, or as others say, heaven and earth, of which the former is the active and the latter the passive principle of things. [*Therefore 365 sects arose.*] which are by no means to be understood literally, but through a certain misuse of words or hyperbole; namely, that as many sects arose as there are days in the year—that is, as if every day a new sect came forth after the fall of the first man and the loss of original justice.

<margin>The Incarnation of the Son of God.</margin>
In the fourth point, it teaches the Incarnation of the Son of God from a Virgin in Judea; it explains the Star going before the Magi with gifts to adore Christ. [*So that the Law and the Prophecies of the 24 Prophets might be fulfilled.*] This alludes to the four major Prophets and fourteen minor ones, to which if you join Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, Job, Moses, Samuel, David, and Zechariah (the father of St. John the Baptist), you will have 24 
<margin>Who the 24 Prophets are.</margin>
Prophets. Then it follows with Christ's descent into hell after death, and His ascension into heaven, having left behind Him 27 Books, which are the New 
<margin>Which are the 27 Books of the New Covenant.</margin>
Testament: that is, the four Gospels, the Acts...

# THE WRITINGS OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

...of the Apostles, the fourteen Epistles of Saint Paul, three of Saint John, one of Saint James, two of Saint Peter, one of Jude Thaddeus, and the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation</note>, which make up the 27 books of the New Covenant.

<margin>The dress of the Priests.</margin>
In the fifth point, he first explains the dress of the Priests, namely that they grew beards and shaved the tops of their heads in the form of a crown <note>The tonsure</note>; the ecclesiastical ceremonies of the earliest times sufficiently show that this was customary for the Priests and Bishops of souls, and this custom is preserved to this very day by many in both the Eastern and Western Church.

<margin>
    The mortification of passions, and the vow of voluntary poverty.
    Common life.
    Fasts.
</margin>
Furthermore, as a testimony to the Holy Gospel, he commends the mortification of passions and disturbances of the soul, equanimity, and voluntary poverty with a contempt for riches; a communal way of living in all things, as was the practice in the primitive Church and is used in modern times in the communal life of Religious orders. He further extols fasting both for the observation of Divine precepts and as a timely, indeed necessary, remedy for restraining the impulses of the flesh.

<margin>The seven hours of Canonical prayers.</margin>
Seven times a day they perform prayers for both the living and the dead. These allude both to the seven Canonical hours and to the prayers instituted for souls detained in Purgatory; entire ecclesiastical antiquity clearly teaches that the suffrages <note>intercessory prayers</note> of the Catholic Church have great power in liberating them from these punishments. For they would pray in vain for souls already enjoying heavenly bliss, since they have already attained their end; in vain for those detained in hell, since in hell no redemption is possible; therefore, it is for souls existing in the purifying fire. Thus, even from this Monument, the Heterodox <note>Protestants/non-Catholics</note> should not be ashamed to learn the consensus of the ancient Church with the modern. And this was the doctrine of the first Christians of both the Eastern and Western Church, continued even unto these

<margin>Purgatory.</margin>
times. So that this may become clearly known to the Heterodox, I shall here bring forward some testimonies of the Chaldeans or Syrians, from which the doctrine of the Syro-Chaldeans—who propagated the Evangelical Doctrine within China—regarding Purgatory is openly shown. First, the Syro-Chaldeans sing thus in the very hymn of the Mass for the dead:

<note>original Syriac: "Dissipate, O Lord, the force of fire and flame by your mercy from the departed who believed in you, and fell asleep and rested in your hope."</note>
*Dissipate, O Lord, the force of fire and flame by your mercy from the departed who believed in you, and fell asleep and rested in your hope.*

<margin>Prayer for the departed.</margin>
And such things do not lack ancient authority, namely Apostolic tradition, which is found both inserted in the Constitutions of various Eastern nations and read in the prefaces of Councils with a title of this kind: *Concerning the departed, and how commemoration is to be made for them, and it is a precept of Paul the Apostle: there are some, however, who say it is a precept of James.* The words themselves are found among the *Melkites and Maronites* in chapter 39, among the *Copts* in chapter 22, and among the *Jacobites* in chapter 5, section 1. The Constitution begins thus:

<note>original Arabic: "Perform on the third day the commemoration of those who have fallen asleep in the faith, and prayer, because Christ died and rose after three days. And let there be for them also nine days for the remembrance of the living and the dead. And perform for them thirty days according to the ancient law, for the children of Israel mourned for Moses thirty days. And let there be performed for them also a yearly commemoration in their stead, and let them give from their wealth alms to the poor. And we believe that this should be done for people of chastity, faith, and goodness; but as for those who are masters of great sins and infidels, it is not..."</note>
*Perform on the third day the commemoration of those who have fallen asleep in the faith, and prayer, because Christ died and rose after three days. And let there be for them also nine days for the remembrance of the living and the dead. And perform for them thirty days according to the ancient law, for the children of Israel mourned for Moses thirty days. And let there be performed for them also a yearly commemoration in their stead, and let them give from their wealth alms to the poor. # CHINA ILLUSTRATED.

-> <note>Arabic: "If all the wealth in the world were given on their behalf, it would not benefit them at all ۞ [Purify them from] sins as a white garment is cleansed of filth ۞"</note> <-

*that is, On the third day, make a commemoration for those who have fallen asleep, with psalms and prayers, because Christ died and rose again on the third day. Let a commemoration also be made for them on the ninth day in memory of the living and the dead; likewise, observe the thirtieth day for them according to the rite of the ancient Law, since the children of Israel wept for Moses for 30 days. Furthermore, let an anniversary be held for them, and let alms be distributed to the poor from their goods. And indeed, we believe this is done for those endowed with self-control, faith, and justice; for as for those who commit greater crimes and the unfaithful, even if all the goods in this world were given as alms, it will profit them nothing.* Here they expressly distinguish venial sin <note>Less serious sins that do not fully rupture the soul's relationship with God.</note> from mortal sin; so that those who depart from this world contaminated by venial sins must endure the purification of fire until the exact cleansing of all venial sins is achieved: but for those who depart hence burdened by mortal sins, no hope remains for liberation from hell. You may see all these matters treated more extensively by Abraham Ecchellensis <note>A famous Maronite scholar and linguist (1605–1664) who taught in Rome.</note>, Professor of the Syriac language at the Roman Athenaeum, in the *Catalogue of Chaldean Books* by Hebedjesu. Certainly, all the Offices and Liturgies for the dead—not only of the Syrians and Chaldeans, but also the Offices, alms, and prayers of the Copts, Arabs, Ethiopians, Greeks, Armenians, and Latins—tend toward this: to liberate just souls from the pains of purifying fire. Indeed, even the <margin>Muslims believe in Purgatory.</margin> Muslims themselves believe this, as is evident from the *Book of Subtleties* (as they title it), in which they are accustomed to pray for the deceased with this context of words:

*that is, O God, make their souls rest, and illuminate their graves, and wash them with water and snow, and cool their heat, and cleanse them from sins, just as a white garment is cleansed from filth.* Thus, even from these few examples, the consensus of all Orthodox Churches regarding Purgatory is clear; for to pray for the dead is the same as believing in Purgatory, so that souls detained there for lighter stains may be liberated from their pains, aided by the prayers of the pious.

### On the Sacrifice of the Mass.

Once every seven days, they perform sacred rites to cleanse the soul from sins. These words imply the Sacrifice of the Mass performed every seven days, that is, on Sundays. The "sacred rites" mentioned here mean the same thing in the Chinese language as "Sacrifice"; from which we can rightly infer that the propagators of the Divine Word in China performed the Sacrifice of the Mass every seven days—that is, the Liturgy, or the unbloody sacrifice of the body and blood—according to the rite used by all Oriental Churches since the primitive Church. <margin>The Sacrifice of the Mass was always offered in the Eastern Church.</margin> Of these Liturgies (according to the cited book by Ecchellensis), more than fifty are numbered among the Orientals, some common and some specific to individual nations: the first and always most highly esteemed was that of St. James the Apostle, according to whose model the rest were later instituted, partly by the remaining Apostles (St. Peter, John, and Mark), Dionysius, the Roman Pontiffs, and the Doctors of the Church. Regarding the Liturgy of St. James, Maro the Syrian <note>A monk and saint from whom the Maronite Church takes its name.</note>, who lived between the fifth and seventh centuries, writes thus:

> <note>Arabic: O God, give rest to their souls and illuminate their graves and wash them with water and snow and cool their heat and purify them from...</note>

> <note>Syriac: This [offering] which is offered for the atonement of souls. And may he live by your body and your blood. And may he be a guide for them to the new life of your kingdom.</note>

-> *that is,* <-

<meta>catchword: that is,</meta>

<summary>Kircher argues that the concept of Purgatory and the Sacrifice of the Mass are universal across Eastern and Western churches, citing Syriac, Arabic, and Chinese traditions as evidence. He uses linguistic analysis and liturgical history to prove that the early Christian missions in China practiced the same sacraments as Rome.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kircher’s argument regarding the ancient presence of Christianity in China, specifically focusing on the theology of the Eucharist and the historical timeline of Chinese Emperors mentioned on the Nestorian Stele.</meta>

->ATHANASIUS KIRCHER<-

that is, <note>original Latin: "Hoc est principium Liturgie Divi Jacobi Apostoli..."</note> *This is the beginning of the Liturgy of Saint James the Apostle, which is the most ancient of all Liturgies, and therefore the others established their own orders according to it.* Since, truly, in the Sacrifice <note>The Mass or Eucharist</note> the true body and blood of Christ are offered, it follows surely that these Orthodox propagators of the Gospel in China, while they performed the Sacrifice every seven days, likewise offered the true body and blood of Christ according to the rite of the Orthodox Eastern Church, continued from the Apostles themselves. For among the Eastern Churches, none but heretics ever had a doubt about such matters. All of this could be proven most extensively from the Liturgies of the Syrians, Chaldeans, Arabs, Copts, Ethiopians, and Armenians, if the matter were not already more than well-known to those skilled in Eastern rites, or if the plan of my current work permitted it. Certainly, Saint Peter, Bishop of Sebaste <note>brother of Basil the Great and Gregory of Nyssa</note>, when asked by infidels whether, while he performed the sacred rites—that is, the Sacrifice of the Mass—he believed he was effecting the true body and blood of Christ? He replied with these words: *When the Priest stands praying before the Altar, or performing the holy Mass, then, once the form of words has been spoken which Christ taught his Apostles and commanded them to hand down to their successors: then, he says,*

> <note>original Arabic</note> And we send down the Holy Spirit upon that
> offering, and the bread becomes flesh
> by its holiness and power, and the mixture
> of wine becomes blood.

that is, *The Holy Spirit descends upon that offering, and makes the bread flesh by its sanctification and power, and makes the mixture of wine and water blood.* And a little later:

> <note>original Arabic</note> But it all becomes flesh
> and holy blood by the Holy Spirit.

that is, *But it becomes sanctified flesh and blood in the Holy Spirit.* Dionysius Bar-Salibi, the Syrian Bishop of Amida, agrees with this in Chapter 1 of his *Commentary on the Liturgy of Saint James*, after he spoke of various kinds of sacrifices, in these words:

> <note>original Syriac</note> Therefore we say that this heavenly bread
> is in truth and in effect the body
> of the Son of God, and it is the same person
> which he took from the Virgin Mary
> and was made a sacrifice for us upon
> the Cross.

that is, *We say therefore that this heavenly bread is in truth and effect the body of the Son of God, and that it is the very body of that person which he took from the Virgin Mary, and which was made a sacrifice on the Cross for us.* All of this is approved by Abdisho <note>Abedjesu</note>, the Chaldean of Babylon, in the 4th treatise of the *Pearls*, chapter 5, *On the Sacrifice*, as cited in Peter Stroza’s book *On the Mysteries of the Chaldeans and the Babylonian Church in union with Rome.* But let these things said concerning the Sacrifice of the Mass suffice.

# Concerning the succession of the Kings of whom this Monument makes mention.

<margin>1. *Tai-tsung*, King of the Chinese, promoter of the Gospel.</margin>
1. The year in which the propagator of the Gospel, *Alopen* <note>Olo puen</note>, first arrived in China from Judea was, according to the Chinese reckoning, approximately the year 636, at which time the King *Tai-tsung Wen-huang* <note>Emperor Taizong of Tang</note> presided over the Empire of the Chinese with illustrious fame.

<margin>2. King *Gaozong*.</margin>
In the year 651, after the death of his father *Tai-tsung Wen-huang*, his son *Gaozong* <note>Emperor Gaozong of Tang</note> succeeded as King of the Chinese; he appointed *Alopen* as Bishop of the Great Law by which the Kingdom was governed.

<margin>3. *Wu Zetian* <note>Xim liè</note>.</margin>
2. In the year 699, when *Wu Zetian* <note>Xim liè</note>, the successor of *Gaozong*, administered the Empire of the Chinese, the Buddhist priests <term>Bonzes: Buddhist monks or clergy</term> of the Pagodas first rose up against the Holy Law in the province of *Henan*; and in the following year, 713, private individuals also persecuted the Law in *Xi'an* <note>Sieno</note>, the ancient royal city of *Wen Wang* <note>Van vam</note>, which some say was *Xi'an Fu* <note>Siganfu</note>, in the province of *Shaanxi* <note>Zenfi</note>.

3. In the year 719, reigning after *Wu Zetian*, there was...

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

<margin>4. King Xuanzong <note>original: "Hiven çum chi taō"</note></margin>
There was King *Xuanzong*, who restored the Law <note>Christianity</note> after it had been oppressed by wicked persecutors. He did this through Bishop John, whose name is also seen inscribed on the margin of the Tablet, and another man of great virtue, *Gile* <note>original: "Kiè liè"</note>.

<margin>The fable of the Chinese Dragon.</margin>
However, what is meant in this passage by the *Dragon's beard* must be explained. The Interpreter and Commentator of this *Chinese* text reports that this refers to an ancient history of the Kingdom, or rather a fable; for they say that one of their Kings sat upon a Dragon flying through the air, whom the King's companions urged forward with their weapons. Those who followed the war, however, plucked the hairs from around the Dragon's beard and held them in memory of their King. This event took such deep root in the minds of superstitious men that, to this very day, the *Chinese* Empire uses no other insignia but Dragons. They not only paint them everywhere, but they also adorn clothes, books, paintings, and whatever monuments of public utility there may be with the figures of Dragons.

<margin>5. King Suzong <note>original: "Ça çum nen meñ"</note></margin>
<margin>Another expedition from the West to China by sea.</margin>
4. In the year 757, King *Suzong* governed the Empire of the *Chinese* with the greatest happiness for the whole Kingdom and the propagation of the holy Gospel. Under this King, another expedition of Apostolic men from *Judea* to *China* occurred through a certain Priest named *Jiho* <note>original: "Kiè hò"</note>, who is said to have reached *China* by the help of the stars—that is, by a maritime route.

<margin>6. King Daizong <note>original: "Tai çum ven vū"</note></margin>
5. In the year 764, King *Daizong* ascended the Imperial throne, likewise an extraordinary propagator of the Divine Law, as is evident from the context.

<margin>7. King Dezong <note>original: "Kien cium xim ven vū"; under him a new expedition from India to China.</margin>
6. In the year 781, *Dezong* ruled the *Chinese* Empire; a just, pious, and beneficent King to all, and a zealot for the Divine Law. Under him, from the most remote regions of the *Pagodas*—that is, from *India*, today the Kingdom of the *Mogul* <note>original: "Magoris"</note>—a new Priest and propagator of the Gospel arrived in *China*. He was received by the King with the highest honor, as is clear from the context of the writing; it was also under him that the inscription of the Stone was made.

And this is the succession of Kings who favored the holy Gospel of Christ in the *Chinese* Empire.

---

## C H A P T E R VI.
->Interpretation of the Syriac names<-
->which occur on the Monument.<-

Now let us proceed to that inscription which is engraved on the margins. Because of the scarcity of people skilled in this writing in *China*, it has remained unexplained until now. For that reason, it was sent by our Portuguese Fathers to *Europe* to be explained by those skilled in the Syriac language. I was the first to undertake its interpretation with no small result, and I have finally delivered it with the appropriate faithfulness and diligence.

<margin>What the Syriac words carved on the Stone contain.</margin>
It is engraved on the margins of the Stone in ancient Syriac letters, which they call *Estrangelo* <note>original: ܐܣܛܪܢܓܠܐ</note>. It contains the year, the names, and the offices of those Apostolic men and Promoters of the Divine Law who were living in *China* at the time this Stone was written and erected. Father *Emmanuel Diaz* mentions this Stone and the aforementioned inscriptions in a certain Portuguese letter dated from *Macau* on August 23, 1625, which I have seen fit to record here in Latin words <note>translated here into English</note>.

> In the Province of *Shaanxi* <note>original: "Xensí"</note> in *China*, he says, where *Trigault* <note>Nicolas Trigault, a Jesuit missionary</note> is currently located, a certain Stone was dug up, about 24 palms in size. On it, it is clearly shown that 1,243 years ago there were Christians there with tonsured heads <note>shaven crowns</note> who preached the mysteries of the Trinity and the Incarnation, and that many Kings of the Chinese bestowed favors upon them. Doctor *Leo*, a Christian, wished the whole matter to be printed, as well as the mysteries shown on that Stone...

# THE WRITINGS OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

<note>Athanasius Kircher (1602–1680) was a German Jesuit scholar and polymath who published this account in his work "China Illustrata" (1667).</note>

...shown by the Christian Mandarins <note>High-ranking officials in the Chinese imperial civil service</note> to be written about, so that the matter might become better known. This is currently being done so that the entire affair may come to the King's <note>The Emperor of China</note> notice; may God turn all things to His glory.

Father Francisco Hurtado, from the Province of Nanjing, confirms this very thing and adds that this year a door was opened into the new provinces of the Chinese Kingdom: Shaanxi, Shanxi, and Fujian, the latter of which borders furthest Cochinchina <note>Modern-day Vietnam</note> and easily admits foreigners. At the end, however, he says the following: <margin>Syriac inscriptions.</margin> *To these were added ten or twelve Syriac lines, which I could not interpret. Also attached were the favors granted by the Kings of China to the Priests of that Law <note>The "Luminous Religion," or Christianity</note>. This version was made word-for-word from the Chinese idiom. Other versions exist, but all agree on the essentials. In the Books of the Kings of China, it is recorded that our faith reached China 994 years ago, and 140 years after its entry, this inscription was made.* Thus says Father Manuel Dias. In another letter, slightly more recent, dated November 21, 1627, from Macau, it is written thus concerning this Stone: *In years past, a certain long and ancient writing was found, carved into a very large stone in the Chinese and Chaldean (or Syriac) languages; from which it is established that the Law of the Lord our God reached China almost a thousand years ago, specifically in the year* <margin>In the year of Christ 636, the Gospel was brought to China.</margin> *of our Lord 636. That stone, however, was erected and inscribed in the year of our Lord 782; that is, 146 years after the preaching of the Law in that place, by which a great conversion to the faith was made, churches were built, and Bishops appointed. The Kings who lived during that time number eight, whose names that stone expresses, all of whom favored the Christians; the Preachers of the Law, moreover, came from Palestine and other neighboring places; it contains the principal mysteries of our faith, etc., which are evident from the inscription.* All these things are extensively clear from the testimonies cited above from Father Boym, Martino Martini, and Daniello Bartoli, collected from the archives of our Society. Truly, I shall perform a worthwhile service if, in this place, I display the Syriac inscription in the same Gospel characters <note>Syriac script</note> in which it was expressed in China, together with its interpretation; especially since our Chinese Fathers sent it to Europe on a particular sheet printed in China to the masters of that language (as we said a little before) for the purpose of explaining it better: for with this explained, I hope it will come to pass that the year, the names, the homeland, and the offices of the Preachers of the Divine Law, as well as our own goal, may be better revealed.

The inscription of Syriac names is twofold; one is carved in the margin, the other at the foot of the Monument; and since the latter contains the year of the erection, we shall begin the thread of our exposition from it, as follows.

### The Syriac Inscription
<note>Kircher provides a transliteration of the Syriac text here.</note>

> *Adam, Priest and Chorepiscopus <note>A rank of regional bishop</note> and Papas <note>Pope/Father</note> of Zinostan <note>China</note>. In the days of the Father of Fathers, the Lord Hanan-Isho, Catholic Patriarch <note>The head of the Church of the East in Baghdad</note>. In the year one thousand and ninety-two of the Greeks <note>The Seleucid era, corresponding to 781/782 AD</note>. The Lord Jizdbuzid, Priest and Chorepiscopus of Kumdan, the royal city, son of the late Milis, Priest from Balkh, a city of Tokharistan, erected this tablet of stone, wherein is written the administration of our Savior and the preaching of our fathers to the Kings of the Chinese.*
>

>
> *Mar Sargis, Priest and Chorepiscopus.*
>
> *Sarnishu, Priest.*
>
> *Gabriel, Priest and Archdeacon, and Head of the Church of Kumdan and Sarag.*

### The Explanation of it is as follows:

Adam, Priest or Presbyter, and Archbishop and Papal [representative] of Zinostan or the Region of the Chinese (where note, that *Stan* in the Indian language signifies "Region," whence *Hindostan* or *Indostan*, *Sinostan*, *Turkistan* or *Turchestan* and similar names signify the same as the Region of the Indians, Chinese, or Turks, not unlike the following German names *Friesland*, *Franconia* [Franckeland], etc., which signify the regions of the Frisians and Franks, and innumerable others of this kind, all of which are composed from the German *Land*, as the Indian names are from *Stan*, namely from the proper name of the place or region—

# The Syriac Inscription explaining the year the monument was erected.

<note>The following text is translated from the original Syriac script provided in the engraving.</note>

In the year one thousand one hundred and ninety-two of the Greeks <note>original: "1192 of the Seleucid era," which corresponds to 781 AD</note>, Yazdbuzid, Priest and Chorepiscopos <term>Chorepiscopos: a country bishop or a deputy bishop acting in rural areas</term> of Kumdan, the royal city, son of the late Milis, Priest from Balkh, a city of Tokharistan, erected this marble tablet, whereon is written the dispensation of our Redeemer, and the preaching of our fathers to the King of China.

Adam, Deacon, son of Yazdbuzid, Vicar of the Bishop.

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

are composed. I have therefore thought it necessary to place them here, so that you might not be ignorant of the reason why China is called *Zindostan* <note>original: "Zindostan"; likely a variant of Hindustan, used here to refer to the regions of the East</note>. In the days of the Father of Fathers, Lord *Hanan Jesua* <note>original: "Hanan Jesua"; Timothy I, a Patriarch of the Church of the East</note>, or *John Joshua*, the Catholic Patriarch (understand this to mean the Patriarch of Alexandria, Antioch, or Babylon, who are properly called universal or Catholic Patriarchs). In the year one thousand and ninety-two according to the Greeks; Lord *Jidbuzad*, Priest and Vicar Bishop of *Cumdan* <note>original: "Cumdan"; the ancient name for Chang'an, modern-day Xi'an</note>, a city of the Kingdom (namely, the Metropolis). Son of *Milis*, may his soul rest in peace, Priest of *Belehh* in the city of *Tahurstan* (read *Turkestan*), erected this Tablet; the Pope (so they call the highest in any Ecclesiastical order) and written upon it are the administration, or governance, of our Savior, and the preaching of our Fathers, who were among the Kings of the Chinese.

And these things are found in the folio printed in *China*, which are also seen carved into the stone.

But before I proceed further here, one difficulty remains to be resolved, which occurs regarding the year of the erection of this Stone. For the year in the Chinese inscription is found to be so different from the Syriac that not a few, perplexed by this diversity, doubted with me concerning the true and faithful interpretation of this Stone. For the Portuguese version from the Chinese, and the Italian from that, place the year of the Stone's erection at 782 after Christ. The Syriac, however, according to the Greek computation, establishes the year 1092. Who does not see how different one is from the other? But so that all these things may be more exactly reconciled, and this knot also untied, I have thought it best to first propose the translations of the said inscription word-for-word.

### *The Portuguese version reads thus:*

<margin>Portuguese.</margin>
*In our Potentate of the great Tam <note>The Tang Dynasty</note>, in the second year of this Kieñ Cium <note>The Jianzhong era</note>, which was the year of the Lord 782, in the month of Autumn, on the seventh day, being Sunday, this stone was raised; Nim ciu being Bishop, who governs the Church of China.*

### *The Italian reads thus:*

<margin>Italian.</margin>
*In our Potentate of the great Tam, in the second year of this Kieñ Cium, which was the year of the Lord 782, in the month of Autumn, on the seventh day, being Sunday, this stone was raised; Nim Ciu being Bishop, who governs the Church of China.*

### *The Interpretation of both*
->*is this:*<-

<margin>Latin.</margin>
" In our Potentate of the great *Tam*, namely the King, in the second year of this *Kieñ cium*, which was the year of the Lord 782, in the month of Autumn, on the seventh day, being the Lord's day, this Stone was exalted, while Bishop *Nim ciù* was administering the Chinese Church.
"

### *The Syriac Inscription reads thus:*

<margin>Syriac.</margin>
<image-desc>A line of Syriac Estrangelo script.</image-desc>

->In the year one thousand and ninety-two of the Greeks.<-

->That is, In the year one thousand and ninety-two, which is of the Greeks.<-

<margin>D.</margin>
Since, therefore, the Orientals, or the Greek Church, for the most part agree with the Latin in the calculation of years, the question is: by what reasoning are these years to be reconciled?

<sig>F 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: I say</meta>

<summary>This page examines the inscriptions on the Nestorian Stele, highlighting a chronological discrepancy between the Chinese date (782 AD) and the Syriac date (1092 of the Seleucid era). It provides translations of the dating formulas in Portuguese, Italian, and Latin to prepare for a technical reconciliation of the two calendars.</summary>
<keywords>Nestorian Stele, Tang Dynasty, Hanan Jesua, Chang'an, Syriac Christianity, chronology, Seleucid era, Jianzhong era, missionary history</keywords>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page continues Athanasius Kircher’s analysis of the Nestorian Stele (the "Stone") found in Xi'an, China. He is reconciling the two different dating systems found on the monument: the Christian era and the "Era of the Greeks" (Seleucid era) used by Middle Eastern communities.</meta>

# OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

<margin>Adjustment of the Greek years to the years of Christ.</margin>
I say, therefore, that this Stone was marked with two types of reckoning used by the aforementioned proclaimers of the Divine Law: one Ecclesiastical, the years of Christ, which the Christian faithful in *China* used in various places; the other Political or civil, which was common to the Syrians, Chaldeans, Arabs, Egyptians, and nearly the entire East. So that you may better understand this, it should be noted that the years of the "Kingdom of the Greeks" mentioned in the *Books of the Maccabees* <note>The biblical books of 1 and 2 Maccabees use the Seleucid era, starting c. 312 BCE.</note> are the same as those elsewhere called the era of the Seleucids, the Syro-Greeks, or the Syro-Macedonians by the Chaldeans; by the Hebrews, the "Era of Contracts" <note>original: *anni Contractuum*</note>; by the Egyptians, the Alexandrian years, or years from the death of Alexander; and by the Arabs <margin>Why Alexander the Great was called *Dhulkarnain*.</margin> **ذو القرنين** *Dhul-karnain* <note>Arabic for "The Two-Horned One."</note>, which is the same as Alexander holding two horns. They call him this either (as *Christmannus* argues) because of the two "horns" of the world—the East and the West—which he subjugated, or as I consider more likely, after the Ram-headed Ammon <note>The Egyptian god Zeus-Ammon, often depicted with ram horns, whom Alexander claimed as his father.</note>, whose son he called himself, or even because of that male goat with which *Daniel* compared him. Finally, they are called the years <margin>The Coptic Era.</margin> of Philip (which we said **تاريخ القبطي** *Al-Battani* <note>Kircher refers to the astronomer Al-Battani, whom he calls Albitegnius.</note> called the "Coptic Era" above). And the epoch of these years begins 12 full years after the death of Alexander the Great, as *St. Jerome* notes from *Eusebius* in his commentary on *Daniel, chapter 9*; he is followed by *Ribera, Torniellus*, and others. Although these years seem to have had a different form and a different start to their months before *Julius Caesar*, by common calculation they now begin on the Calends of October <note>October 1st.</note>. The names of the months are either Greek or Syro-Chaldaic; in other respects, they agree with the length and form of the Julian year, except that the intercalary day <note>The leap day.</note> in this era is added at the end of the month *Shebat*, which corresponds to our February.

With these things noted beforehand, we establish the beginnings of the Kingdom of the Greeks with *Eusebius, Scaliger*, and others in the 310th year before the Christian era, during the 117th Olympiad according to the *Alexandrian Chronicle*. Indeed, it is well-acknowledged that Alexander the Great died in the year the 114th Olympiad began, in the final days of the month Hecatombaeon, as *Plutarch* writes—namely, 322 years before the era of Christ (for Christ was born in the third year, exclusive, of the 194th Olympiad). Therefore, the beginning years of the Kingdom of the Greeks differ from the passing years of Christ by 310, so that in the first year of Christ, the 313th year of the Kingdom of the Greeks began. From this it follows that if 310 is added to a given year of Christ, the Alexandrian year <margin>The Alexandrian Year.</margin> emerging in that year of Christ arises; if 310 is subtracted from the Alexandrian year, the year of Christ in which that Alexandrian year opens remains. Finally, if the Alexandrian year is subtracted from 310, the remaining number will indicate the year before the era of Christ in which that Greek year began; or when the beginning of the Era of Alexander was, <margin>The Era of Dhulkarnain.</margin> **تاريخ ذي القرنين**, as the Arabs and Egyptians call it.

These things having been thus demonstrated: If you subtract 310 (which is the difference between the Christian and Alexandrian years) from the Alexandrian or Greek year 1092 inscribed on the Stone in Syriac letters, there will remain 782. This is the year of Christ inscribed on the Stone in Chinese numbers, exactly corresponding to the Syro-Greek or Alexandrian year.

### Paradigm of the Calculation.

<margin>Summary of the calculation of the years of this Stone.</margin>
| Chronology of Events | Olympiad / Year | Index |
|----------------------|:----------------:|:-----:|
| Alexander the Great died, according to *Plutarch*, in Olympiad | —— 114. | A. |
| After whose death, according to *St. Jerome*, after 12 years had passed, the Alexandrian era begins, namely in Olympiad | —— 117. | B. |
| Christ was born in the third year (exclusive), namely in Olympiad | —— 194. | C. |
| The Stone was erected in the Alexandrian year | —— 1092. | D. |
| And in the year of Christ | —— 782. | E. |

<summary>Kircher synchronizes the two dates found on the Nestorian Stele—the Seleucid year 1092 and the Christian year 782—by explaining the historical origins of the "Era of the Greeks" and its relation to Alexander the Great.</summary>
<keywords>Chronology, Nestorian Stele, Alexander the Great, Seleucid Era, Dhul-karnain, Olympiads, Syriac, Chinese Christianity, 782 AD</keywords>

--- Page 75 ---
<meta>This page continues the mathematical and historical analysis of the Nestorian Stele's dating, following the calculations from the previous page regarding the Greek and Christian calendars.</meta>

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED.

With these values established, subtract number *B* from number *C* and you will have the difference of the said *Olympiads* <note>The Olympiad was a four-year period used by the Greeks for dating; the author is synchronizing these with the Christian era</note>, namely *77 Olympiads*. When these are multiplied by the 4 years that constitute each individual *Olympiad*, 308 years are produced. To these, add the 2 completed years of the *Olympiad of Christ's birth*, and you will make 310. When these are subtracted from 1092, they leave *E*, the number of the years of *Christ* in which this *Stone* <note>Referring to the Nestorian Stele, a 781 AD monument documenting early Christianity in China</note> was erected. Therefore, they recorded this *Stone* with a twofold kind of years with great care, so that those foreigners who might not understand the year of *Christ* might at least recognize the *Alexandrian* year <note>The Era of the Seleucids, widely used in the Near East</note>, as it was more broadly known.

The proposed difficulty having now been resolved, let us now return to the *Stone*. On its margin, besides those *Syriac* names which we set forth just a little while ago, many other proper names are still seen which are missing from the said leaf, the number of which runs to nearly 70; these we have thought should be placed here.

---

->* * *<-

From these things it is evident that the *Syriac* or *Chaldean language* <note>The author uses "Chaldean" to refer to the Aramaic dialects used by Eastern Christians</note> was common in those first times to the *Churches* of *Syria*, *Palestine*, *Egypt*, and *Babylonia*; indeed, it was even the vernacular in the time of *Christ*. Thus, it is not surprising that the *Gospel of Christ* was carried by the ministers of the word of *God* from the said places not only into the most remote *Regions of the East*, but also into the neighboring *Regions* of *Babylonia*, *Egypt*, and *Ethiopia*, immediately after the *Apostles'* circuit into the whole world. There, as if in the original seats of the *Churches*, the *Syriac* or *Chaldean* language took such root that they consequently sowed it through the universal coasts of *India* and, as has been said, into the furthest of *Regions*, *China*. But more on these matters in what follows.
