## CHAPTER I.

->*By what means, by whom, and by which routes, at various times, the Sacrosanct Gospel of Christ was carried into the farthest Regions of the Orient: India, Tartary, China, and the other Regions of Asia.*<-

---

<margin>Palestine, Egypt, and Greece.</margin>
<image-desc>Large decorative initial 'C' with intricate floral and scrollwork designs.</image-desc>
Since numerous traces of the Christian Religion still exist to this day in *India, China*, and other regions of *Asia*, as has already been shown, I shall make it worth the effort—now that the Syrian-Chaldean Monument found in *China* has been explained—to also show in this place by what occasion and route these Apostolic men penetrated into such remote tracts. For once this is established, it will be clearly evident that *Syria, Egypt, and Greece* were at all times the nursery not only for promoting the Christian Religion in foreign regions, but also, before Christ, for the propagation of every superstition throughout the entire world. However, so that I may proceed more clearly in an obscure matter, it seemed best first to set forth the opinions of certain men here concerning these things; so that, having discussed them, what ought to be determined regarding the proposed matter may be more brightly revealed.

First, therefore, there are those who assert that these colonies of Christians penetrated into *China* and the other regions of *Asia* primarily through the help of that great Asian Emperor, whom authors everywhere call Prester John <note>original: "Presbyterum Joannem." A legendary Christian patriarch and king popular in European chronicles from the 12th to the 17th centuries.</note>. But since there is a very great controversy among authors concerning this Emperor and the location, quality, and condition of his kingdoms, we shall first of all bring forward some points concerning him and his kingdoms for a clearer explanation of things, even though we have also discussed this subject in the *Coptic Forerunner* <note>original: "Prodromo Copto." Kircher's 1636 work on the Coptic language and Egyptian culture.</note>.

### Who was that famous Prester John, and did he ever exist?

Just as the Kings of the *Egyptians* were first called *Pharaohs* and later *Ptolemies*; the rulers of the *Moors* are called *Sherifs*; and the *Persians* were once called *Xerxes* and *Artaxerxes* but are now called *Sophi*; so the name of *Prester John* <note>original: "Presbyteri Joannis"; a legendary Christian patriarch-king of the East</note> has for a long time denoted the dignity of certain *Christian* Princes. The more learned experts agree that these men ruled in *Asia*, and not among the *Ethiopians* or in any other part of *Africa* as many wrongly believe; however, the exact location where he held his empire is not entirely discovered.

<margin>Where Prester John might have lived.</margin>
For what some have written—that they were the Kings of *Cathay*—makes the matter more doubtful, since in these recent years it has been learned that whatever is *Cathay* belongs to the *Chinese* <note>original: "Sinas"</note>, and there is no city or Kingdom called by this word that is now found outside the borders of *China*. And in this all the Fathers of our Society <note>The Jesuits</note> agree, who stayed in *China* for many years and are most highly instructed in the knowledge of geographical matters: *Matteo Ricci*; *Nicolas Trigault*; *Alvaro Semedo*; *Michael Boym*; *Martino Martini*; *Johann Grueber*; *Johann Adam* <note>Johann Adam Schall von Bell</note>, that great Mandarin of the *Chinese* Royal Court; and finally *Benedict Goës* of the same Society, who set out from the Kingdom of *Mogor* <note>The Mughal Empire</note> by order of his superiors to industriously investigate *Cathay* by a land route, and explored it sufficiently. I would not believe it far from the truth that, besides that part of the land which was found by our men to be within the limits of *China* under the name of *Cathay*, there is another much larger region of the world, bordering the Kingdom of *China* to the North and West, which also formerly had the name *Cathay*. But since that entire region outside the *Chinese* walls is for nearly a two-month journey a desert, uncultivated and destitute of inhabitants, it is likely that the region of *China* was in later

times properly called *Cathay*, as it is most cultivated and fertile in all things; while the desert today called *Kalmuk*, outside the walls and the regions bordering it, formerly deserved the name of *Desert Cathay*. In the neighboring kingdoms of this place, he of whom we now treat, Prester John, held sovereignty in the age of our ancestors. *Marco Polo the Venetian* calls it the Empire of the Great *Khan*, but the Holy Scriptures, according to the testimony of *Arias Montanus*, call it *Gog and Magog*; of whom the *Sibyl* also says:

->Alas, alas to you Gog, and to all in order<-
->together with Magog,<-
->Marson and Angon, how many evil<-
->fates approach you.<-

<margin>Arabian Geography.</margin>
Furthermore, *Arabian Geography* teaches through four entire later climates <note>A "climate" was a geographical zone in ancient and medieval cartography</note> that this is *Scythian Cathay*, and hands down to men that it is filled with animals and minerals, flourishing with Christians, and surrounded by the immense districts of the *Caucasus*, called *Jagog and Magog*. Thus relates the *Arabian Geography*, part 9, climate 5, line 21; the words translated by us from Arabic into Latin <note>and here into English</note> sound thus: *From the lodging of Garada to the city of Tahamet on the Southern side, four leagues are completed (one of which is 25,000 paces); from the city of Garada to the mountain which is called Caucasus, it is a journey of seven days. And this mountain surrounds the Regions of Jagog and Magog;*

<margin>The Caucasus and its height.</margin>
*the mountain indeed consists of sides so steep that no one can cross them, and if one should ascend, he will never reach the summit of the mountain, hindered by the abundance of ancient snows and thickened ice; and because these never melt, they present the appearance of a certain cloud perpetually sitting upon the Caucasian peaks at the top of the mountain. Beyond the mountain, however, are many cities of Jagog and Magog. And it contin-*

# 48 ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

It happens not infrequently that men of this region, led by curiosity, ascend to some height to see what is at the summit of the mountain and what lies behind it, and they do not return. Their return is impossible, either because they are torn apart by wild forest beasts, or because they are captured by the other peoples dwelling beyond the mountain. Those who occasionally return unharmed report that in the transmontane region <note>the land across the mountains</note> many fires are seen at night, but by day nothing else but a mist mixed with darkness. Again, in part 7 of the said climate <note>a "climate" was a geographical zone in ancient and medieval cartography</note>, line 34: In these regions many Christians, or Nazarenes, also dwell. And in part 8, line 18, he says a great force of gold is collected from mountains of this kind, along with various kinds of precious stones and hunts of wild beasts; finally, that a great Prince presides over these regions. All of which fits the Empire of the Great Khan, or Cathay, as the eyewitness observer Marco Polo the Venetian <note>original: "Paulus Marcus Venetus"</note> relates in book 1, chapter 64, in these words:

<margin>Marco Polo the Venetian.</margin>
Going from the Notable Province toward the East, the journey leads to Tenduc (better called Tanchut). (Now, Tanchut is a Kingdom of Tartary <note>a historical name for North and Central Asia</note> which encompasses many other Kingdoms, such as the Kingdom of Lhasa—or what the Tartars call Barantola—the Kingdom of Nepal <note>original: "Necbal"</note>, Tibet, Maranga, etc., as will be shown later, together with the desert of Kalmykia <note>original: "Kalmack"</note> which the Chinese walls terminate; and most geographers confuse this Kingdom with Cathay). In this kingdom are many cities and forts, where that great King, most famous in the whole world and commonly called Prester John <note>a legendary Christian patriarch and king popular in European chronicles</note>, was also accustomed to stay. Now, however, that Province pays tribute to the Great Khan, having a King from the lineage of Prester John.

<margin>Tenduc, Lhasa, Barantola, Nepal, Maranga, Tibet, Kingdoms of Tartary.</margin>
And although there are some Idolaters and Muslims there, nevertheless the greater part of the Province observes the Christian faith, and these Christians hold the primary positions in this Province. Especially there is a certain people there called the Argon Province, who are wiser and more eloquent than the rest of the people. Here also are found the Regions of Gog and Magog, which they name Lug and Mongug. In these places is found Lapis Lazuli, from which the best azure is made. There are also in these mountains of the great Province silver mines and manifold hunts of forest animals. All of these things aptly agree with the Arabic description cited a little earlier. Concerning the height of the mountains of Gog and Magog, he writes thus in book 1, chapter 37:

<margin>M. Polo the Venetian.</margin>
If you depart from here to the Eastern region, you must ascend for three continuous days (namely the arduous cliffs of the Caucasus) until you reach the highest Mountain, than which there is none higher in the World. No bird appears there because of the cold and the excessive elevation of the land, which can provide no food for animals.

<margin>Mount Belor, the highest mountain.</margin>
<margin>Ineffectiveness of fire on the mountain.</margin>
If a fire is lit there at any time, because of the excessive coldness of the region, it is not so bright, nor so effective in its action, as in lower places. And a little later: And this Region is called Belor, bearing the likeness of winter at all times, until the traveler completes a 40-day journey. Thus says the Venetian Marco. All of these things agree with that mountain which they today call Langur, the highest of all, existing in the Kingdom of Lhasa;

<margin>Mount Langur is the same as Belor.</margin>
concerning which Father Johann Grueber <note>a Jesuit missionary and explorer who traveled from Beijing to Rome via Tibet</note>, who also measured it by a journey on foot, reports that to cross its summit in the summer time is not without danger, both on account of the extreme thinness of the air, which hardly allows travelers to breathe, and also on account of the evaporation of a certain poisonous herb, which kills both men and beasts with its breath. And around this Kingdom of Belor—the ancient station of the Sacae—both the Arab Geographer and the treatise called *The Paths of the World* <note>original Hebrew: "Orhot Olam"</note> by Rabbi Abraham Farissol <note>original: "Abraham Pizol"</note> clearly demonstrate that Tibet, the principal Kingdom of Prester John in Cathay, is situated.

<margin>R. Abraham Farissol.</margin>
The Nubian Geography calls it Begarger, in which it asserts there is a very great city in the following words: *In the Eastern part of it is the Region of Begarger, whose greatest city is named Centaba, equipped with twelve gates of iron.* Rabbi Farissol, along with the Venetian, calls it Belor, in which he says the Kingdom of Tibet was recently discovered; his words are these:

<meta>Catchword: The Kingdom</meta>

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

<note>original Hebrew: מלכות באלור הגדולת... The Hebrew text provided is a translation of the Latin description of the Kingdom of Belor, likely included by Kircher to demonstrate his polyglot expertise.</note>
The Kingdom of Belor is great and exceedingly high according to all those historians who have written of it; many Jews are enclosed within it, and it lies on the North-Eastern side. There are also other Eastern peoples recently discovered in the same Kingdom, whom the natives call Tibetans. Near to this is a city, surpassing all other cities in size, almost divine; there is none like it under heaven, in which all good things are found. This can surely be no other than the city of Chaparangue <note>Modern Tsaparang, the capital of the ancient Kingdom of Guge</note> situated in the Kingdom of Tibet. Concerning the monuments of Religion left there formerly by Christians, Father Antonio Andrade, a Portuguese of the Society of JESUS, relates wonders. When he heard that its inhabitants were Christians, he undertook an expedition full of labor and difficulty from the Kingdom of Mogor <note>The Mughal Empire</note> in the year 1624. Having also discovered the sources of the Ganges and the Indus, he observed many things most worthy of consideration and admiration, as was confirmed to me by his companion on that journey, Joseph of Mogor, a Christian. While I am writing this, Joseph—still robust and vigorous despite being 85 years old—is in *Rome* with Father Heinrich Roth, the leader of the New Christians in Mogor,

<margin>The city of Chaparangue.</margin>

---

and he has related each and every detail to me by word of mouth.

<margin>The origin of the Ganges in the mountains of Tibet.</margin>
In the highest mountains of *Tibet*, white with perpetual snow, there is a huge lake, the water-reservoir of the greatest rivers of all *India*, from which the *Indus*, *Ganges*, *Ravi*, and *Attock* take their beginnings. The *Ganges*, cast down through the highest rocks with a horrific noise, begins its course in a very deep valley; the *Indus* and the other rivers obtain their exit through the roots of the mountains, as is clear from the map.

He asserts, moreover, that this Kingdom is one of those contained within Great *Cathay*, both outside and inside the Chinese walls, although this is not entirely consistent with the journey undertaken by our *Benedict Goës* to *Cathay* by official labor and the command of superiors, as will be seen later.

And in these vast Regions of *Cathay*, I find on the testimony of *Marco Polo the Venetian* that the most powerful Emperor, Prester *John*, held seventy-two *Kings* under his dominion, partly Christian and partly Heathen. However, the similarity of the Kingdoms in those vast Regions, and the different naming of places resulting from the various revolutions of nations, the tumults of wars, and other vicissitudes of things, has produced such great confusion that to this day there is no one who can extricate himself from it.

<margin>Confusion of the names by which Kingdoms are called.</margin>

For some everywhere confuse him with the Great *Khan*; others call him *Ascid*, a *Persian* by origin; so says *Al-Makin in book 3, chapter 4 of the History of the Saracens*:

<note>original Arabic: كل من ملك فرغانه... This Arabic quote explains the title of the rulers of Fergana.</note>
> Every King of Fergana (as they call the city of Sogdiana) is called *Ascid*, just as the Emperor of the Romans is called *Caesar*, or the Persians *Chosroes*. There are those who, with a name more recent than ancient, identify him with the Ethiopians...

<meta>This page continues Kircher’s investigation into the origins of the name "Prester John," specifically debating whether the title belongs to an Asiatic or African monarch.</meta>

# THE WORKS OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

where they call him የዮሐንስ፡ በለል፡ <note>original: "Juchanes Belul"</note> that is, **Precious John**. Some assert, by a not improbable conjecture, that he was called **Joanam** among his people in honor of the Prophet Jonah, a name common to all who held that Empire. However, in these Western parts of the Latin Church, it was customary to call him **John** with the addition of the title **Presbyter** <note>The term "Presbyter" means "Elder" or "Priest."</note>; not because he was a priest, but because, in the manner of a primate Archbishop, he saw to it that an erect Cross—by which he proclaimed himself a defender of the Christian religion—was carried before him. From this, **Scaliger** <note>Joseph Justus Scaliger (1540–1609), a famous scholar of chronology and languages.</note> thinks he was so named from the Persian word فرشتگانی <note>original: "Prestegiani"</note> ***Prestegiani***, which sounds like "Apostolic." He suggests that Westerners, misunderstanding these words, wrote ***Presbyter Joannes*** <note>Prester John</note> instead of the word ***Prestegiani***. I subjoin his words:

> Indeed, we have wondered more than once how a nation entirely ignorant of maritime affairs was so powerful by land and sea that it extended the borders of its Empire from Ethiopia all the way to China; for from those times, knowledge of that Emperor reached us, but under the name ***Prestegiani***. In the Persian language, which holds a place throughout almost all of Asia as Latin does in the West, this signifies "Apostolic," by which name it is certain a Christian and Orthodox King is understood.

For in Persian, فرشتگانی ***Prestegiani*** is the plural for "Apostles," but فرشتگانی ***Prestegiani*** [singular] is "Apostolic," just as پادشاه فرشتگانی ***Padischa Prestegiani*** is said for **"Apostolic King"**; in Arabic it is ملك الرسولي ***Melek Arresuli***, and in Ethiopic ንጉሥ፡ ሐዋርያዊ፡ ***Negus Havarjavi***. *That the Empire of the Ethiopians themselves in Asia was great and wide is indicated by the Ethiopic Crosses seen in Japan, China, and elsewhere; moreover, the Temple of Thomas the Apostle situated in the region of Maabar <note>The Coromandel Coast of India.</note> has nothing if not Ethiopic crosses, structure, and many other things, and—what you might wonder at—the name itself.* Thus far Scaliger. From this discourse, it is indeed well gathered that certain colonies were transferred from Ethiopia into India, China, and other parts of Asia to propagate the Christian faith in those places, which is what we are now striving to show.

<margin>Prester John was never in Africa.</margin>
But as for his saying that Prester John was African by origin, or that he settled in Ethiopia after being driven out of Asia to take power, this is entirely fabricated. As we shall see later, it lacks all foundation; for a great part of the better authors agree with me that the African and Asiatic Emperors were different people. Indeed, the Empire of the Asiatic one flourished for many years until it came to a certain David. As **Marco Polo the Venetian** <note>The famous explorer (c. 1254–1324).</note> relates, he was conquered in battle by a certain leader called **Genghis** <note>original: "Cinge"</note> (who, having been elected Emperor by the **Scythians** <note>The Mongols.</note>, began to be called **Un-Khan** and **Naiam** instead of Presbyter). This defeat, which occurred under his uncle **Kublai**, brought a great loss to the Christian cause and put an end to both the glory of the Empire and the name of Prester John, as we have shown from the *history related above from Marco Polo*.

<margin>Why the Emperor of the Abyssinians was called Prester John.</margin>
However, I shall make clear by what error the name of Prester John was given to the **Abyssinian** <note>Ethiopian</note> Emperor. At the time when the **Portuguese** <note>original: "Lusitani"</note> were searching for new regions of the earth by sea routes, the name of Prester John was celebrated with great fame throughout Europe. He was said to be a most powerful Emperor, lord of many kingdoms, and a Christian by religion; yet where he was located remained unknown. Therefore, when **Pêro da Covilhã** <note>original: "Petrus Covillanius"</note>—whom **King John II of Portugal** had sent to investigate that Prince, first by the Mediterranean Sea and then by land—had arrived in Asiatic India, he heard that in that Ethiopia which lies below Egypt, there was a certain prince by far the most powerful who professed the Christian religion. He betook himself to him; and since he found many things in his presence which agreed with the constant rumors spread among Europeans regarding the true Prester John, he believed that he was the one called Prester John. He, therefore, was the first who began to call the **Abyssinian** Emperor by that name, that is, ***Preste Jean***.

<summary>Kircher discusses the etymological origins of the name "Prester John," arguing it stems from a Persian word for "Apostolic" and explaining how the title was mistakenly transferred from an Asiatic monarch to the Emperor of Ethiopia by Portuguese explorers.</summary>
<keywords>Prester John, Ethiopia, Abyssinia, Joseph Scaliger, Marco Polo, Pêro da Covilhã, Genghis Khan, Persian language, Apostolic King, Church history</keywords>

--- Page 83 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion regarding the true identity and location of Prester John, distinguishing the Asian monarch from the Ethiopian Emperor.</meta>

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

<margin>Prester John, a most powerful King of Asia.</margin>
...to call him by that name; since then, others who entered *Ethiopia* in subsequent years followed suit and easily introduced the same error into *Europe*. All of this is learnedly taught by *Father Balthasar Tellez* in his *History of Ethiopia*, written in a polished *Portuguese* style. The most wise Patriarch of *Ethiopia*, *Alphonso Mendes*—a great light of our Society <note>The Society of Jesus, or the Jesuits</note>—also subscribes to these views in a certain letter concerning *Ethiopian* affairs prefixed to the work of *Father Tellez*. Therefore, when we speak of *Prester John* <note>A legendary Christian patriarch and king popular in European chronicles from the 12th to the 17th centuries</note>, we by no means understand him to be the *Abyssinian* <note>Ethiopian</note> Emperor. Aside from the fact that his Empire is separated from the *Asian* one by the whole span of the heavens, there is also no mention in the Chronology of the Kings of *Ethiopia* (as is clear from the Latin version held in the *Vatican*) of any migration from *Africa* into *Asia*, or from the latter to the former. Indeed, *Damianus à Goes* expressly denies in his book *On the Customs of the Ethiopians* that he is called *Prester John*, or was ever called so, which the aforementioned *Ethiopian* priests also assert. Rather, we understand

him to be that greatest Prince of *Asia*, of whom we have spoken at length in the preceding pages. And certainly, even to this very day, traces of that formerly great *Prester John* remain in the Kingdom of the *Tangut* Empire—which the Tartars call *Barantola*, the Saracens call *Boratai*, and the inhabitants call *Lhasa*. Our Fathers *Albert d'Orville* and *Johann Grueber* (who passed through there when returning from *China* to *Europe* in the year 1661) testify sufficiently to this. They report marvelous things concerning the 

<margin>The King of Barantola is adored as a God, called "Patis."</margin>
superstitious adoration of this King. There are two Kings in this realm: the first is occupied with the administration of the whole kingdom; the other—whom they call the God the Father, or the Heavenly Father, or also the Grand Lama, or the High Priest of the sacrificers—resides in the innermost recesses of his palace like a deity, receiving ceremonies of divine worship from everyone. They hold him in such high honor that they foolishly persuade themselves that even his filth and excrement are highly conducive to curing all manner of infirmities; hence, they not only mix these into medicines but are not ashamed to carry them in a box hung around the neck. Truly, because a corruptible man cannot escape the bonds of death, his worshippers—lest it be said that he perished like other men—have devised a plan (taught to them by their master, Satan). They search the entire kingdom for a Lama as similar to him as possible; when they find him, they elevate him to the throne of the Eternal Father by a clandestine plot. Thus, just as this fabricated deity is most similar to the previous one, he can also appear to have truly risen from the dead—indeed, he is said to have already risen seven times. The reader will understand the rites and ceremonies with which the foolish people follow him in perpetual veneration more fully in the following pages. They say, moreover, that these ceremonies and worship flowed from no other source than that King whom authors everywhere call *Prester John*, and they are certain that he resided in the Kingdom of *Tangut*, which includes the Kingdom of *Lhasa* in its domain. For just as people used to travel to him from all over *Tartary* as if to an Oracle, so even now they do not cease to flock from all sides to this ridiculous "God the Father" and head of the Lamas to obtain his blessing.

<margin>The veneration of the Tartar-Chinese Monarch toward this Prelate.</margin>
This reached such a point that in the year 1629, even that great *Tartar* Monarch of the *Chinese* <note>The Qing Emperor</note>, as soon as he gained power over the *Chinese* Empire, was urged by the *Tartar* magistracy (following the custom received from his predecessors) to perform homage to the Grand Lama—that is, to the Priest—as the supreme head of the *Tartar* religion. Matters had progressed so far that the King, led by the advice of his counselors, had fully decided to meet the Grand Lama (who was already moving toward *China*) at a distance of nearly a two-month journey, even to the very walls of the *Chinese* Kingdom. He would have done so had he not been turned away from such an indignity by *Father Johann Adam* <note>Johann Adam Schall von Bell, a Jesuit advisor to the Qing court</note>, a close associate of the King, through the great weight of the arguments he brought forward. The Emperor profited so much from the instruction of the said Father that he was far from...

<summary>Kircher argues that Prester John was an Asian ruler located in the region of Tibet (Lhasa/Tangut) rather than Ethiopia, identifying the Grand Lama as the modern successor to this legendary figure's religious status. He describes the "superstitious" succession of the Dalai Lama and mentions how Jesuit influence prevented the Qing Emperor from performing a humiliating homage to him.</summary>

# OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

Far from deigning to meet him outside the city and its walls, the Emperor did not even grant him an audience except in the garden of the palace in Beijing. Finally, following the usual custom, he dismissed him to his own kingdom laden with various gifts, but having neglected all the ceremonies usually granted to him. Thus, he who was thought to be about to fill the Empire of China with his arrival and blessing found that exactly the opposite happened to him; so much so that after his departure, that ridiculous High Priest <note>original: "Archifacrificulus" - a diminutive, mocking term Kircher uses for the Dalai Lama</note>, cursed by everyone everywhere, seemed to have left behind him famine, plague, war, and every kind of curse.

<margin>The Seat of Prester John in the Tartar Kingdom of Tangut.</margin>
But to return to our purpose: I say, therefore, that it is not far from the truth that Prester John <note>A legendary Christian patriarch and king popular in European chronicles from the 12th to the 17th centuries</note> resided in this Kingdom of Tangut. For while he and his successors ruled far and wide in Asia and outstandingly promoted the Christian faith, preachers of the Divine Word—either invited, or of their own accord due to the fame of those kingdoms and zeal for promoting the faith, or what is more probable,

<margin>Driven into exile by fear of persecution, they finally reached China.</margin>
at the time of that dire persecution which first began under the rule of Diocletian and Maximian <note>Roman Emperors known for the "Great Persecution" of Christians, c. 303 AD</note>, when followers of the Christian life were sought out everywhere for the most cruel punishments in Syria, Egypt, and the neighboring places of Ethiopia—it is entirely likely that, fleeing from said places driven by fear, they settled in the middle of Persia, Bactria, and Turkestan, and then gradually reached the farthest regions of Asia through a long exile. Furthermore, in the year 600 after Christ, under the rule of Heraclius, when the treacherous Muhammad was coming into power, new and ever newer colonies of Christians were led into the said regions of the East. Certainly, the Syrian or Babylonian priests, yielding to the fury of the infidels, did not cease to propagate the Christian religion over the course of time, as the Syriac inscription cited above clearly teaches, where Izbuzad, a Chinese Bishop, is called the son of Noah, a certain priest originating from the city of Balkh in the Kingdom of Turkestan;

<margin>Izbuzad, Priest and Vicar of the Bishop, was a Turkestani by nation, born in the city of Balkh.</margin>
just as is evident from the Syriac words mentioned above and repeated in this place: *In the year one thousand and ninety-two etc. Jydbuzad, Priest and Vicar of the Bishop of the royal city of Kumdan, son of Noah the Priest, originating from the city of Balkh in the Kingdom of Turkestan etc.* That Turkestan is the region which the Persians call Uzbek, the beginning of Great Cathay, and that the city of Balkh is situated in it, the Arab Geographer <note>likely referring to Al-Idrisi</note> clearly shows in part 8, climate 4, line 34, where, treating the peculiar province of the Khwarezmian Turkestanis, he establishes the site of the city of Balkh—once a most celebrated city—not far from Samarkand, the royal court of the Great Tamerlane, in these words translated by me from Arabic into English <note>original: "ex Arabico in Latinum"</note>:

> *This is the eighth part of the fourth climate, and it contains a portion of the Khwarezmian region; and from Karman to Kasaralkamat is 15 miles; from here to Samarkand is 6 miles, and there is a road for those coming from Samarkand into the city of Balkh.*

From which it clearly appears that Jydbuzad, called the Vicar of the Bishop, was Syrian by origin but Turkestani or Khwarezmian by homeland. That many others also penetrated into China from the region of the Pagodas and other regions of Asia for the sake of propagating the Gospel is shown from the aforementioned Syrian-Chinese stone inscription <note>The Nestorian Stele, discovered in 1625</note>, all of which we leave to the curious reader for further scrutiny. And let these remarks suffice concerning the colonies led into the Kingdoms of Prester John and into China.

Furthermore, those who believe that most of these evangelical men first arrived in India from Syria, Egypt, and Ethiopia, and that India was converted to the faith of Christ by their labor, and that they subsequently sent out certain colonies into China because of the frequent commerce that existed at that time between the Indians and the Chinese by sea route—these people hold an opinion that is as true as it is certain, the sequence of which expedition I shall briefly make clear.

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

## CHAPTER II.

### Concerning the Propagation of the Gospel by St. Thomas the Apostle and his successors throughout all the Regions of Eastern Asia.

It is well known that among the various regions of Asia brought under the rule of Christ by the preaching of St. Thomas the Apostle, one is that vast transmontane district of India which, extending from Cape Comorin to Narsinga and Bengal, looks out toward the side of the famous island of Ceylon. In this region, the said Apostle converted an innumerable multitude of men to Christ (who to this day are called "Thomas Christians" after St. Thomas) and also shed his own blood. The Malabar Annals <note>The "Malabar Annals" refer to the traditional historical records of the Saint Thomas Christians of Kerala, India.</note> record that this occurred in Meliapore, the royal city and metropolis of Narsinga, although some claim he suffered in "Calamina," a distinct city of India, or as others mistakenly suggest, in Salamis in Cyprus. It will become clear that this error crept in from a poor understanding of the word "Calamina" once we reveal what place Calamina actually was.

<margin>The place of St. Thomas's martyrdom.</margin>
Note, therefore, that according to the Malabar Chronicle, no city of this name is found in India today, nor was it found in the past. Instead of "Calamina," one should read *Calurmina*. This word is known to be Malabaric <note>original: "Malabaricam," referring to the Malayalam language or the local dialect of the Malabar Coast.</note>, composed of *Calur* and *mina*; the latter signifies "upon" and the former "rock," as if to say "Upon the Rock." Furthermore, in that place in Meliapore where the Holy Apostle is said to have been affected by martyrdom, there is a certain huge rock which to this day they call *Calur* in the Malabar language.

Therefore, while the Malabar Calendars record that he suffered at *Calurmina*, they by no means intended a particular city by that word, but rather the rock near Meliapore called *Calur*, which the Apostle was accustomed to climb daily for the sake of prayer, and upon which, while he was intent on prayer, he was later killed. Hence, if anyone asks one of the Thomas Christians where the Holy Apostle died, they will answer nothing other than *Meliapore calurmina*, that is, "at Meliapore upon the rock." This was related to me orally by a man most worthy of trust, Father Peter Paul Godinho, a Portuguese Rector of the College of the Society of Jesus at Cochin in India, who arrived in the city as the Procurator of the Malabar Province while I was writing these things. He asserted that the Malabar Annals hold this to be true, and it is believed so by the unanimous consent of all Christians. Likewise, the miraculous Cross itself bears witness; appearing miraculously on a certain stone, it was imprinted by the flow of the Holy Apostle's blood while he was shedding it. To this day, it is preserved with the highest devotion by the natives in the Church of St. Thomas at Meliapore. Indeed, this is taught even more clearly by an inscription carved around the Cross itself in an unusual type of character for a perpetual memorial of the event. I have seen fit to attach this here, taken from the *Xaverian History* of Father João de Lucena, together with the mystical characters of the Brahmans <note>original: "Brachmanum," referring to the priestly caste of India; the author is likely referring to Sanskrit or a local South Indian script.</note>.

# OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

### Mystical characters written around the circumference of the Cross.

<image-desc>A rectangular box containing four lines of mystical, calligraphic characters. These characters represent the Tamil or Malabaric script mentioned in the text. They are stylized with various loops, overhead strokes, and complex forms, arranged in four distinct rows.</image-desc>

The Brahmins <note>The priestly caste of India</note> assert that these were mystical characters of the ancient Philosophers, and that each one corresponds to a specific word, not unlike the mystical signs of the Egyptians; although others more probably think that each corresponds to a single syllable, as is evident from the Alphabet of the Tamil language <note>original: "Tamul"</note> and the syllabary of those same letters. Indeed, the explanation made by a certain Brahmin and translated from the Malabar language into Portuguese is reported by the previously cited Lucena <note>Joannes Lucena (1550–1600), a Jesuit historian</note> in the following words.

<margin>
Interpretation of the writing from Father Joannes Lucena.
</margin>

> After the law of the Christians appeared in the world, thirty years from then, on the 21st of the month of December, the Apostle St. Thomas died in Meliapore <note>Mylapore, India</note>, where there was knowledge of God and a change of the law, and the destruction of the devil. God was born of the Virgin Mary, remained in her obedience for thirty years, and was an eternal God. This God taught his law to twelve Apostles; one of them came to Meliapore with a staff in his hand, and built a church; and the King of Malabar, and he of Coromandel, and he of Pandi, and various other nations and sects all determined of their own free will, agreeing among themselves to subject themselves to the law of St. Thomas, a holy and penitent man. The time came that St. Thomas died by the hand of a Brahmin, and from his blood this Cross was made.

> In the thirtieth year, on the twenty-first day of December, after the promulgation of the Christian law was made throughout the whole world, the Holy Apostle Thomas died in Meliapore; from whom [the city] also holds the knowledge of God, the change of the Law, and the destruction of the Demon. God was born of the Virgin Mary, and lived under her obedience for 30 years, and was God without end. This God taught his Law to 12 Apostles, from whose number one arrived at Meliapore holding a staff in his hand (others say a carpenter's rule and a stake: it is said this stake, cast up from the sea onto the shore, was of such vastness that many elephants were not sufficient to move it from its place, which nevertheless the Holy Apostle—having received it from the King as a gift for the foundation of a church to be established—tied with his own belt and, by the power of Christ and the Holy Cross, is said to have dragged it no differently than the lightest straw) and built a Church. The King of Meliapore and of Coromandel and of the Pandi and others of various nations and sects (Princes) all resolved with a ready will to eagerly submit themselves to the Law of St. Thomas (having seen a very great miracle). The time came when St. Thomas would fall by the hand of a certain Brahmin, and from his own blood this Cross was pressed out. Thus far Lucena.

<margin>
Miracles of the Cross.
</margin>

Furthermore, this Cross, on the 18th of December—namely the feast of the Blessed Virgin which the Spaniards call the "Expectation of the Birth" <note>The Feast of the Expectation of the Blessed Virgin Mary</note>—is accustomed every year at the time of Solemn Mass to change itself into various colors, and even occasionally...

thence to emit blood with a most copious sweat; which, however, experience has taught was always a prognosis of great imminent calamity. We have thought it necessary to place here the true image of the Cross together with the Characters <note>The inscriptions or symbols on the monument</note>.

### The Miraculous Cross of St. Thomas the Apostle at Meliapore in India.

The figure of a Peacock overhanging the Cross is the insignia of the King of Narsinga. As for the other things which we said are accustomed to happen around this Cross at certain times, which are altogether miraculous, read in the aforementioned *Lucena* and *Osorius*, whom *Baronius* cites in *volume 1, year of Christ 57.*

These things might perhaps seem outside our scope; but since I have happened upon them by some chance, I did not wish to pass them over in this place, both as a monument of extraordinary antiquity perhaps not so well known to Europe, and so that I might reveal to the Reader—in a digression perhaps not unwelcome—how the Breviary and Martyrology <note>Church books containing lives of saints and daily services</note> regarding the place of St. Thomas's passion should be understood. Therefore, let us return to the path we left.

<margin>Martome the Syrian restores the failing Church.</margin>
<margin>Year 1500 <note>original: "S 1500 tome"</note></margin>
And so this Region of transmontane India, converted to Christ by St. Thomas the Apostle, after it had persisted constantly in the received faith for many years, at length, as laborers and ministers of the Word of God failed, the Region gradually fell away from its former fervor and imbibed various errors of the nations; whence the whole Religion was brought into extreme danger, and was not far from going to ruin—and indeed would have gone—had not Divine providence, taking pity on the many evils of the failing Church, opportunely come to its aid through the work of a certain devout man, a *Syrian* by nation, who was commonly called *Martome*, that is, Lord Thomas. For he, by a singular instinct from God, set out from Syria to India, and when he beheld this vineyard so uncultivated, squalid, and bristling with thistles and thorns, out of the supreme care with which he was touched for propagating the Christian faith, he judged it must be tilled by him. First, having won over the minds of the natives both with benevolence and by the very great authority in which he excelled (for he was no less distinguished by the splendor of his birth than by his learning, erudition, and fame), he soon appointed Bishop-coadjutors summoned from *Syria* and *Babylon* (either *Chaldea* or *Egypt*) throughout different places of the Kingdom. He introduced the Syriac Language, in which the Apostle had formerly preached the holy Gospel in that same Region, as tradition held; finally, he omitted nothing of those things which could contribute to the growth of the Church. By this means the vineyard of Christ, restored to its former state, made such gradual progress that, no longer content within its own borders, it extended its most fertile branches into all *India*, and even as far as *China*.

<margin>Syrian Priests infected by the Nestorian heresy.</margin>
But it happened at length in the course of time, that these Syrian Priests, infected by the stain of the Nestorian heresy <note>A theological doctrine later declared heretical, emphasizing the disunion of Christ's human and divine natures</note>, themselves shamefully destroyed with perverse dogmas whatever their ancestors in India had laudably accomplished in the propagation of the Christian Religion. So the said *Annals of Malabar* testify; so do the *Portuguese writers of the Indian Annals, Joannes de Barros and Diogo do Coutto*; so finally reports *Father Luis de Guzmán in his history of the Indian expeditions written in the Spanish language, book 2, chapter 27*, whose formal words I have decided to add here for greater attestation of the faith. For he writes thus:

> *There are many Christians in India with this name of St. Thomas, who exceed one hundred and fifty thousand, although distributed through various kingdoms, and subject to various pagan and Moorish <note>Muslim</note> Kings. These Christians have their Archbishop, Bishops, and Priests, who come from Syria, and commonly the Oriental Patriarch of Babylonia (or Alexandria) provides them; and because the Bishops cannot come to visit these people except from time to time, so that they do not lack Priests, they ordain the children, while they are very small, to all orders, although they do not exercise them until they are grown. The cause of these Bishops and Priests having entered—*

<meta>catchword: and Priests-</meta>

3 Syrian Priests in India, and having such influence and authority with the Christians of St. Thomas, there was a man named Martome the Syrian, which in the Syrian language means the same as "Don" <note>A Spanish title of respect</note> in Spanish. Martome was a very wealthy and prominent man, and for this reason, he had access to the Kings of Cranganore and Quilon. Because of the name he shared with Thomas, and his authority in that land, the Christians descended from the Apostle St. Thomas began to join him. He became like a head over them, protecting and favoring them, and in this way, he won their hearts. Since he was Syrian, he easily persuaded them to receive and admit Bishops from that land, making them believe that Syrian was the language in which Christ our Lord had preached in Jerusalem; and consequently, that the Syrian Bishops taught the true doctrine of the Gospel, the same that the Apostle St. Thomas had preached to their ancestors. In this manner, the Syrian Bishops first entered the Kingdoms of Cranganore, Quilon, and Cochin, and little by little they spread to all parts, even as far as China.

> "That is to say: There are many Christians in India,
> "whose number exceeds nearly one hundred and fifty thousand,
> "called Thomasine Christians, divided through various Kingdoms,
> "found subject to different Kings of the Gentiles and Moors. They have
> "their own Archbishop, Bishops, and
> "Priests, who all come from Syria,
> "usually designated for this kind of expedition by the Babylonian or Alexandrian Patriarch. But because the Bishops
> "cannot visit such remote lands except very slowly and rarely,
> "lest they be destitute of Priests, it is the custom for small boys to be initiated into orders, even if they do not
> "exercise their functions until
> "a more advanced age. The reason why the Syrian Bishops and
> "Priests, having entered India, gained such great authority for themselves among the said Christians, was a certain man
> "of the Syrian nation, named Martome, which
> "in our language signifies Lord Thomas:

this Martome was exceedingly wealthy and distinguished, and for this reason, he had entered into favor with the Kings of *Cranganore* and *Quilon*; and by the occasion of the name of Thomas, and the great authority which he possessed, the Thomasine Christians joined themselves to him. Thus Martome, having become as it were their head, so bound the minds of all to himself by his benevolence, that it was not difficult to persuade them to receive and admit only Bishops from Syria into India. The Syrian language was very powerful in persuading them of this, as he demonstrated it to be the same language which Christ our Savior once used in Jerusalem, and in which the Holy Apostle Thomas had formerly promulgated the sacred Gospel to their ancestors. By this reasoning, therefore, the Syrian Bishops first entered the Kingdom of *Cranganore*, *Quilon*, and *Cochin*, and gradually occupied all the surrounding regions even unto China.

Thus says *Guzman* <note>Luis de Guzmán, a Jesuit historian</note>. All of which is confirmed by the above-cited *Father Paul Godinho*, who says that to this day they know no other doctrinal language besides the Syrian; that they keep all these monuments and all sacred and Ecclesiastical books written in it; and finally, that all literate men, especially those to be initiated into sacred orders, are bound to learn this language just as we are bound to learn Latin. He adds that at *Vaipicota* <note>A seminary in Kerala</note> in the College of the Society of JESUS, it is taught professionally as being necessary for converting the *Brahmins* <note>The priestly caste of India</note>. These are the principal traces we have discovered both in *India* and in the Kingdom of the *Chinese*; certainly, we can trace the origin of the Christian matter in these Kingdoms deeper from those things which we have managed to collect from the *Chaldean* <note>Syriac</note> books of the *Malabar* coast. That this coast was joined to Christ by the labor of Saint Thomas the Apostle is clearer than can be called into doubt even by the most stubborn critics.

<margin>Syriac books in India hand down many things concerning St. Thomas.</margin>
In those books, therefore, we read most clearly that the faith of Christ was brought by the same Apostle, and many Churches were established in that Kingdom. And lest such a great matter should perhaps seem doubtful to anyone, I shall provide the testimonies of the books themselves, translated word for word from the Chaldean into

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

I shall here set down the Latin translation <note>original: "Latinè reddita"</note> of those things which Father Giovanni Maria Campori—a member of our Society <note>The Society of Jesus, or Jesuits</note>, a laborer in that vineyard for many years, and highly skilled in the Chaldean language—translated by order of the Reverend Archbishop Father Francisco Ros, the Pastor of that same Church and also of our Society. At our request, he transcribed them with his own hand so they might be included in these Commentaries, lest such a remarkable monument of antiquity should ever perish. The matter, therefore, stands as follows.

<margin>The Chaldean Breviary of the Malabar Church.</margin>
In the Chaldean Breviary of the Malabar Church of Saint Thomas, which is called the *Gaza*—that is, the *Treasure*—in the office for *Saint Thomas the Apostle* during the second Nocturn <note>A division of the night-time prayer service</note>, it is recorded word-for-word in one Lesson:

->The following words are extracted from the original Chaldean.<-

I. Through Saint Thomas, the error of idolatry vanished from the Indians. <note>original Syriac: ܒܝܕ ܡܪܝ ܬܐܘܡܐ...</note>
II. Through Saint Thomas, the Chinese and Ethiopians were turned to the Truth.
III. Through Saint Thomas, they received the Sacrament of Baptism and the adoption of sons.
IV. Through Saint Thomas, they believed in and confessed the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.
V. Through Saint Thomas, they preserved the faith they received in one God.
VI. Through Saint Thomas, the splendors of life-giving Doctrine arose in all of India.
VII. Through Saint Thomas, the Kingdom of Heaven flew and ascended to the Chinese.

->that is,<-

1. **Through Saint Thomas, the error of Idolatry vanished from the Indians.**
2. **Through Saint Thomas, the Chinese and Ethiopians were converted to the Truth.**
3. **Through Saint Thomas, they received the Sacrament of Baptism and the adoption of sons.**
4. **Through Saint Thomas, they believed and confessed the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.**
5. **Through Saint Thomas, they preserved the faith of one God which they had received.**
6. **Through Saint Thomas, the splendors of life-giving Doctrine arose for all of India.**
7. **Through Saint Thomas, the Kingdom of Heaven flew and ascended to the Chinese.**

Then, in a certain Antiphon, it is stated: *The Indians, Chinese, Persians, and other Islanders, and those in Syria, Armenia, Greece, and Romania, in commemoration of Saint Thomas, offer adoration to Your Holy Name.* Furthermore, in the summary of the **Synodal Canons**, part 2, discourse 6, chapter 19, concerning the established Canons regarding Bishops and Metropolitans <note>A bishop with authority over a large province</note>, there is also the Canon of Patriarch Theodosius in these words: *These six seats are the heads of the provinces and the Metropolitans, namely Hilam, Nisibis, Prath, Assur, Beth-Garmai, and Halah, who were deemed worthy to assist in the ordination of the Patriarch; they must not be absent, so that others may meet with the Patriarch every fourth year. Likewise, the Bishops of the great Province—namely the remaining Metropolitans of China, India, Pales, the Maufsaeans, Xan, the Raziguans, Herion (this is Cambay), and Samarkand (this is Uzbekistan and the land of the Mogor <note>The Mughal Empire</note>)—who are very far away, and whom vast mountains and turbulent seas do not permit to travel at will, shall send letters of agreement (that is, of communion) to the Patriarch every sixth year.* Indeed, when the Portuguese arrived at Cochin, **Lord Jacob** ruled this Church of the Malabar mountains, and he used to write thus:

->Metropolitan of India and China<-
->[METROPOLITAN OF INDIA AND CHINA]<-

as is also evident from his own manuscript codex of the New Testament, where it is stated at the end: *Lord Jacob, Metropolitan of India and China, wrote this book.* In the same manner, **Lord Joseph**, who succeeded the aforementioned **Lord Jacob** and died at Rome, used to sign his name: *Lord Joseph, Metropolitan of all India and China,* and this is the most ancient title of the Bishops of this Church.

# THE WORKS OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

From this I conclude without doubt that the authors of such remarkable expeditions to the farthest borders of *Asia* were none other than the aforementioned Syrian Bishops of the followers of St. Thomas. All of these were called Syrians indiscriminately, either because they broadly called that entire tract—which extends from the Euphrates all the way to the Erythraean Sea <note>The ancient name for the body of water including the Red Sea and the Indian Ocean</note>, along with the nearest and farthest shores of said sea—Syria or Assyria; or because of the Syrian or Chaldean language, which all the more learned men profess. We have shown this above regarding the Arabian, Egyptian, and Ethiopian monks, and Benjamin <note>Benjamin of Tudela, a 12th-century Jewish traveler</note> demonstrates this regarding the Sinai monks in his *Itinerary* with these words:

<margin>
The Bishops of the Church of St. Thomas were from Chaldaea and Syria.
</margin>

> And from there it is a journey of two days to Rephidim, where Arabs dwell, and there are no Israelites there; but from there one goes in one day to Mount Sinai, and on that summit is a shrine of the monks who are called Syrians; at the foot of the mountain, however, is a large fortress which they call Tor Sinai, and its inhabitants speak in the language of the Targum <note>original Hebrew: "leshon targum," referring to Aramaic/Chaldean</note>, and they are under the yoke of Egypt.

That these monks were Copts <note>Egyptian Christians</note> is testified by the inscriptions found in the Coptic books made in the monasteries of this Desert Arabia; consider these as they are set forth in the Catalog of Coptic Books. It is clear, therefore, that the Coptic-Ethiopian Church (which encompassed all of Syria in the manner described, and subjected it to the single Patriarch of Alexandria or Cairo) at all times transported new colonies first into India, and from there into China and the other regions of *Asia*. For since *Egypt* held all the best and most convenient ports of the *Erythraean Sea*, and from the *Erythraean* there was a convenient passage to *India*, there was at all times a very frequent coming and going. This is sufficiently declared, among others, by that most famous maritime expedition of Solomon from *Ezion-Geber*, a port of the Red Sea, into the region of *Ophir*.

<margin>
Convenient navigation from the Erythraean Sea to India.
</margin>

But what *Ophir* properly is in this context, I find to be quite controversial among interpreters. I know that some take *Ophir* to mean pure gold itself, but their opinion has long since been exploded. Others, however, designate by *Ophir* that region of *America* which is commonly called *Peru*; since it is twofold, Northern and Southern, they conclude from the following text that they were called by the dual number *Parvaim*: **and the gold was the gold of Parvaim.** <note>original Hebrew: "v'hazahav zahav parvaim" from 2 Chronicles 3:6</note>

<margin>
What kind of region Ophir properly is.
</margin>

<margin>
Ophir is not Peru.
</margin>

So says Arias Montanus; but I do not see how the said fleet could have reached the Antipodes of Palestine—a world entirely and diametrically opposite, disjointed, and unknown to mortals—safely and in such a short time without the magnet <note>the compass</note>, the use of which they were ignorant of at that time, and lacking the other aids by which our master-navigators discovered that world. Furthermore, with what intent would they have attempted to seek gold, gems, and precious woods with such great danger in regions so remote, when they abound in much nearer places, such as the *Golden Chersonese* <note>The Malay Peninsula</note> and *Ethiopia*? Since I do not see how these things could have been done, I rightly judge that opinion to be incongruous and to be exploded. Therefore, about to take a more certain and secure path, I say that the word *Ophir* is Coptic or Egyptian, by which the ancient *Egyptians* called that *India* which contains the Kingdoms of *Malabar*, *Ceylon*, the *Golden Chersonese*, and *Sumatra*, the *Moluccas*, and the *Javas* which belong to it...

<margin>
The Egyptian word Ophir denotes India.
</margin>

<summary>Kircher argues that the "Syrian" Christians in Asia were linked to the Coptic Church of Egypt. He also refutes the theory that the biblical Ophir was Peru, arguing instead that it refers to India and the East Indies based on Coptic etymology and ancient navigation limits.</summary>

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED

The other neighboring gold-bearing islands, to which we read that Hiram dispatched Solomon's fleet at appointed times, bear abundant witness to this. It was not only gold, gems, precious stones, and wood that the fleet of Hiram brought back with it from these regions, which are filled with such things, but also peacocks, monkeys, and the like. Indeed, our Coptic-Arabic *Onomasticon* <note>A vocabulary or dictionary of names.</note> clearly teaches this in the chapter on the Names of Nations; where **Hend** <note>original Arabic: اصحفير عند</note> is explained as *India*, and **Indum** <note>original Arabic: اصحفير هند</note> as *Indian*. Therefore, *Ophir* is nothing other than *India*; and as for the gold of *Parvaim*, I believe it came from **Javaim** <note>original Hebrew: יאוים</note>, that is, from the *Javanese* Islands; for I remember reading in the works of Rabbi Benjamin <note>Benjamin of Tudela, a 12th-century Jewish traveler.</note>, if I am not mistaken, that these islands were called by that double name.

<margin>Expedition from Egypt and Ethiopia through the Red Sea into India.</margin>
Since, therefore, expeditions from the Erythraean Sea <note>The ancient name for the Red Sea and Indian Ocean.</note> to Ophir or India were so frequent, it is certain that not only the propagators of the Christian religion took advantage of this opportunity, but also the ancient Egyptians, their priests, and philosophers had great mutual commerce with said regions, as various monuments left by the Egyptians in those places testify.

<margin>Hormuz.</margin>
Among these is *Ormuz* or *Hormuz*, a most noble city of the Persian Gulf, the first colony of the Egyptians in Persia and a convenient station for those wishing to cross into India. Haithon <note>Hayton of Corycus, a medieval historian.</note> records in his book *On the Tartars*, chapter 6, that it was built by the Egyptian Hermes (from whom it also takes its name, for **Hormoz** <note>original Arabic: هرمز</note> is the same to the Arabs as Hermes is to the Greeks, namely Mercury). Furthermore, Aelius Spartianus asserts that the Kings of Persia were called *Psammosires* after Osiris; and not without reason; for Diodorus <note>Diodorus Siculus, a Greek historian.</note> records in the following words that Osiris first went into those parts, and then into India, and having built cities, brought the people to a better standard of living:

> Then he made his journey through Arabia along the Red Sea, as far as the Indians and the ends of the inhabited world. He founded not a few cities among the Indians, one of which he named Nysa, wishing to leave a memorial of that place in Egypt where he was raised. He also planted ivy in the Indian Nysa, and this plant remains in that place alone of all the regions in India and the neighboring country. He left many other signs of his presence in that land, by which the later Indians were persuaded to claim the God as their own, saying that he was of Indian origin.

From here he proceeded through Arabia, along the Red Sea to the Indians and the very ends of the habitable world. He founded many cities for the Indians, one of which he called Nysa, wishing to leave a monument of that Egyptian Nysa where he had been nurtured. In this Indian Nysa he planted ivy, which grows and remains only in that spot out of all the places in India and the neighboring land. Moreover, he left many other signs of his arrival in those lands, by which later Indians were led to stir up a controversy over this God, contending that he was Indian by nationality. It is very likely that later generations, following in his footsteps, more frequently visited these Egyptian regions. Indeed, the Magi of the Persians, the Brahmans of the Indians, as well as the other wise men of Asia, received their entire method of philosophizing and all their rites and ceremonies concerning the worship of the Gods from the Egyptians through mutual commerce; Philostratus clearly teaches this in his *Life of Apollonius*;

<margin>Worship of the Gods in the Eastern regions of Asia from the Egyptians.</margin>
indeed, the very morals and customs of the said nations clearly indicate it; since to this very day in India, China, Japan, Tartary or Cathay, and even in America itself, we see the worship of the Sun, Moon, Stars, Earth, and animals of every kind flourishing, just as it did in Egypt from time immemorial. You may find everywhere there the many-formed statues of Osiris and Isis, and also Apis bulls, Anubis figures, Canopic jars, and similar Egyptian monsters being worshipped: mystical and magnificent pyramids...

# THE WRITINGS OF ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

most magnificent Temples were erected to Spirits <note>original: "Geniis"</note> according to the *Egyptian* standard; in a word, you may see an *Egyptian* face of things everywhere. I would declare all these matters here more at length, were it not for the fact that I have already treated them most extensively and in great detail in the *fourth Treatise of the first part of our Oedipus* (which I call the *Egyptian* Mimicry <note>original: "Simiam Aegyptiacam"; Kircher refers to how other cultures "aped" or imitated Egyptian rites</note>); for this reason, we refer the Reader to that work. And these are the things which I have thought necessary to say thus far concerning the colonies of the Copts or *Egyptians*; regarding which I am not unaware that others may perhaps feel differently. Truly, these things seemed more probable to me, and I could have brought forward other not-to-be-despised reasons for the proposed matter, if either the method of my undertaking had required it, or if it were in my interest to violently extort assent from the Readers. It is enough for us to have clearly demonstrated in this place—even if perhaps more lengthily than was proper—that expeditions were made from *Egypt* into the whole World, which was our sole aim.

## CHAPTER III.

### *Concerning Cathay and its proper and genuine location.*

<margin>Cathay is nothing other than China.</margin>
Although in the preceding pages we have cited various opinions from various Authors—Latin, Hebrew, Arabic, and Persian—concerning the location of Cathay, here, for a better elucidation of the matter, we shall explain what Kingdom *Cathay* finally is, and where it is situated, through various Journeys undertaken for this purpose.

That *China* is the most significant part of *Cathay* has become known not only through the diligent exploration of our Fathers <note>The Jesuit missionaries</note>, but is also most clearly evident from Marco Polo the Venetian; for that vast city which he calls *Cambalù*—or as it sounds in the true language of the *Tartars*, *Cambalek* <note>Khanbaliq, the "City of the Khan"</note>, the *Royal Residence* of the Great *Khan*—our Fathers say was none other than the modern *Royal Residence of the Chinese*, which they call *Peking*. The vastness of the walls, laid out in a square, and the incredible size of the city demonstrate this more than sufficiently. 
<margin>Description of the city of Cambalù.</margin>
According to the testimony of Marco Polo: *The City of Cambalù, situated in the Province of Cathay near a great river, has been distinguished and royal since ancient times; moreover, Cambalù means the City of the Lord. The Great Khan transferred this city to the other side of the river; for he had learned from an Astrologer that it was destined to be rebellious against the Empire. The City, constructed in a square, encompasses 24 miles in its circuit, with each side of the square having six miles. It has whitened walls, 20 paces in height and ten in width, though that thickness becomes thinner as it rises. Each side of the wall has three principal gates, which in total are twelve, with magnificent palaces established near each one. In the corners of the walls are also remarkable palaces where the arms of the City are kept. Very straight streets and ways pass through this City, so that from one gate a free view lies open through the City to the opposite gate, with most elegant houses built like palaces on both sides.* And a little later: *Outside the City of Cambalù there are twelve large suburbs, contiguous to the twelve individual gates, in which merchants and foreign men are constantly found.* All these things match the Royal city of *Peking* so well that they differ in almost nothing, as *Father Martino Martini* testifies on *page 29 of his Atlas*. Also the names of the cities *Tadinfu*, *Casanfu*, *Quelinfu*, *Cingianfu*, and *Sianfu*—which even 
<margin>Siganfu besieged by the Tartars.</margin>
now is called *Siganfu* <note>Xi'an</note>, situated not far from the Yellow River—which, after a three-year siege, the Great *Khan* finally conquered in the year 1268 by the help of catapults (which machines, previously unknown in *China*, he had ordered to be built by Christian craftsmen). Marco Polo the Venetian (who, together with his father Niccolò and his uncle, was staying with the Emperor at that time) was present. See Marco Polo the Venetian, *Book 2, Chapter 58*. Indeed, *fu* in the Chinese language means nothing other than a large city, just as it is added to other names...

# CHINA ILLUSTRATED.

<margin>The Astronomy of the Cathayans corresponds to that of the Chinese.</margin>
...added to the names, denotes a medium-sized city; these terms are known and unique to no other region but *China* alone. However, nothing demonstrates so clearly that *China* has always been regarded as *Cathay* as the *Persian* monuments, both Astronomical and Botanical. These were revealed to us from the astronomical tables of *Nasir al-Din* <note>original: "Nasirodim"</note>, a Persian mathematician most celebrated throughout the entire East, by the learned man *Jacob Golius* in his *Appendix to the Atlas of China*. There, it is clearly seen that the names of the twelve hours into which the natural day is divided among the *Chinese* or *Cathayans* correspond so perfectly to the *Chinese* names that they do not differ in any respect, as is also testified by those of our Fathers who have come here to *Rome* as visitors. But so that these things may be more clearly evident, I will set down the names here.

<margin>Golius in the Appendix to the Atlas of China.</margin>
### Names of the hours into which the natural day among the Cathayans, that is, the Chinese, is divided.

| | 1. | 2. | 3. | 4. | 5. | 6. |
|:--- |:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Chinese.** | Çu | cheù | yin | mao | xin | su |
| **Arabic.** | صو | چون | يم | ماو | خيو | ثرة |

| | 7. | 8. | 9. | 10. | 11. | 12. |
|:--- |:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Chinese.** | v | vi | xin | yieu | siò | hai. |
| **Arabic.** | وو | وي | شن | يوو | سيو | حاي |

Indeed, if anyone compares the 60-year cycle—which *Nasir al-Din* says the *Cathayans* use in their calculation of days, weeks, and solar years—with the Chinese cycle, I find that the method of computation and the names by which they are accustomed to call it are exactly the same. Let the same judgment be held regarding the Cathayan year, which they divide (as they do the Zodiac) into 24 parts, beginning the year from the 15th degree of Aquarius. Truly, concerning these matters, see the above-mentioned *Golius* more extensively in the cited place; he also brings forward the Persian astronomer *Ulugh Beg* <note>original: "Vlug Begum"</note> as a witness, from whose tables he clearly shows, through the harmony of the *Cathayan* (or what is the same, *Chinese*) calculations, everything that has been handed down thus far. I also read among the Arabic physicians that the musk found in *China* is commonly called مسك كطاي *Cathayan Musk* <note>original: "Muscus Cataicus"</note>, and that drink which is made from the herb *Cha* <note>tea</note> peculiar to *Cathay* is mostly called چا كطاي *Cathayan tea* <note>original: "Cataium cha"</note>. Furthermore, I read that the *Moguls* <note>Mogores</note>, who are *Tartars* by origin and trace their lineage from the Great *Genghis Khan* <note>original: "Cingis can"</note> and the other succeeding powerful Emperors, have been called *Mogul-Chinese* <note>original: "Mogulo-Sinas"</note> by their historians (مغولة سين) since the conquest of the *Chinese Empire*.

<margin>What Quinsai was, and the infinite vastness of its people.</margin>
As for what he <note>Marco Polo</note> writes concerning *Quinsai* (or *Quinfai*), a city of admirable and almost incredible magnitude, all those things are found today to be true of the city of *Hangzhou* <note>original: "Hancheu"</note>, the Metropolis, about which *Father Martino Martini*, the author of the distinguished *Atlas*, discusses as follows on page 109:

<margin>Description of Hangzhou or Quinsai.</margin>
> *Lest European Cosmographers wander any longer in searching for and ridiculously sketching the Quinsai of Marco Polo the Venetian, I will give here that very city itself, of which that "Cosmic Archontology" <note>A contemporary geographical reference work</note> has not attained even a shadow. And if God favors my prayers, I will perhaps in time transmit the "Theater of Chinese Cities" itself, printed by the Chinese themselves long before Europeans, and never before seen in Europe as far as I know. But to deal with what is now at hand, I prove by the most certain arguments that this city is the Quinsai of Marco Polo the Venetian; for this is the one which is five days' journey from Singui (that is, Suzhou <note>original: "Sú cheù"</note>), if we speak of the progress and march of an army, in which it is known Marco Polo the Venetian was; otherwise, it is scarcely a four-day journey. This is the city which in his time was the royal seat of the Chinese, which the Chinese in the learned language of the literati call Kingfu, and the common people speaking less exactly call Kingfai, from which the Venetian name Quinsai flowed.*

<margin>The Etymology of Quinsai.</margin>
*It should further be noted here that "Kingfu" is a title of dignity common to royal cities, and not unique or singular to one, since it truly signifies "Royal City," although often the same city has a specific name of its own, just as this city is called Hangzhou, which under the Song <note>original: "Sunga"</note> family was called Lin'an, because of that family—*

# ATHANASIUS KIRCHER

The tenth Emperor of the Song dynasty, Gaozong <note>original: "Caoçungus"</note>, fleeing the Jin Tartars, established his Royal Court here, and for that reason it was called Kingfu in the time of the Venetian <note>Marco Polo</note>, whatever others may think on the matter; these events took place around the year **1135** from the birth of the Virgin. In this same city, the Song family held the Kingdom until the great Han Western Tartars drove the Eastern Jin Tartars out of Cathay—that is, from the Northern Provinces—and having thus defeated them, they soon carried their victorious arms into the Kingdom of Mangi, beginning the occupation of the Southern Provinces.

<margin>A city filled with innumerable bridges.</margin>
But to come closer to the matter itself, this is that city which possesses almost innumerable very high bridges, both within the city itself and outside in the suburbs; nor will the ten thousand bridges enumerated by the Venetian be far from the truth, if you include the triumphal arches among the bridges. Because of their vaulted appearance, the Venetian could have counted them as bridges, in the same way that he called Tigers "Lions," even though the latter are not found here, nor indeed in almost any part of Asia. Unless, perhaps, you prefer to think he included not only those within or outside the city, but the bridges of this entire territory; in which case he could easily have increased that number of his, which otherwise seems difficult for Europeans to believe, so great is the abundance of bridges and triumphal arches everywhere.

<margin>Lake Sihu.</margin>
To confirm this, there is the *Lake of 40 Italian miles, called Sihu* <note>West Lake</note>, which although it is not within the walls, nevertheless laps against them in a long stretch from the West to the South. From this lake, many canals have been led into the city itself; furthermore, its banks are so crowded on every side with temples, monasteries, palaces, museums, and private houses, that you would believe yourself to be inside a vast city rather than in the countryside.

<margin>The highly cultivated shores of the Lake.</margin>
In addition to this, the banks are everywhere built up with squared and cut stone, leaving a very wide path for the convenience of walkers. Certain roads also cross through the Lake itself, equipped with several high bridges under which ships may pass; those walking on these can go all the way around the Lake, which is why they could easily be reckoned as part of the City by the Venetian. This is that City which has a mountain within its walls on the Southern side called *Chinghoang*, upon which stands that tower with guards where the intervals of the hours are measured by a water-clock <term>clepsydra: an ancient device for measuring time by the flow of water</term>, and for each hour a tablet is displayed inscribed with golden letters a foot and a half long. This is the very city whose streets are all paved with squared stones; this is the one which lies in a marshy place and is divided by many canals capable of carrying ships. Finally, to pass over the rest, this is the city from which the Emperor fled to the sea via the great river *Qiantang* <note>original: "Cien-tang"</note>, the width of which exceeds a German mile, and which flows to the Southern part of the city; thus a river is not entirely lacking here, which the Venetian ascribed to his *Quinsai*. From there, it breaks out toward the East into the sea, 

<margin>The circumference of the City of Quinsai or Hangzhou.</margin>
from which this city is exactly as far distant as the Venetian claimed it to be. I add that the circuit of the city occupies a hundred or more Italian miles, if you count the vast suburbs which extend very widely in every direction; from which you will cover fifty Chinese *li* <note>stadia</note> by walking straight from North to South through the most crowded streets, in which you will find nothing empty of people and houses; one may travel from West to East in almost the same manner. Since, therefore, from Chinese history the time, name, description, size, and all other things demonstrate this city to be *Quinsai*, no room for doubt remains. Thus says Father Martino Martini in the cited place.

### §. I.

### The Journey into Cathay or China by Benedict Goës of the Society of Jesus, from Father Nicolas Trigault. See the Map of Itineraries.

I shall explain in a few words whence such a great confusion of opinions regarding the genuine location of Cathay arose. It is known both from the History of Marco Polo the Venetian and Hayton the Armenian, and even from the Chronology of the Chinese themselves, that the Great *Khan* <note>original: "Cham"</note> or Emperor of the Tartars—whom some call *Kublai*, others *Ulcam* or *Uncam*—in the year **1256**, having broken through the walls, occupied the entire Empire of the Chinese (which was then divided into two 

<margin>China called Cathay by the Tartars:</margin>
Empires, one of which toward the North was called *Cathay*, the other toward the South *Mangi*); whence, as already mentioned elsewhere, the Northern part of China with the rest...

regions outside the walls were commonly called by the name *Cathay*, so too, once the Empire of the *Chinese* <note>original: *Sinarum*</note> had been occupied, the entire Kingdom—through the influence of the neighboring *Tartars* and *Saracens*—began to be called by the name *Cathay*. The names of the other surrounding Kingdoms outside the walls were deleted, so that from 그 time onward, only that vast Empire of the *Chinese* enclosed within the walls was called *Cathay* by those who traveled there annually for the sake of trade from *Hindustan*, *Uzbek*, *Hami* <note>original: *Camul*</note>, and other Mediterranean regions; this is clearly evident from the journey of our Brother *Bento de Góis*. after 15 days *Jaconich*, and after five days he reached *Yarkand*, the metropolis of *Kashgar* and the end of the *Kabul* region; the entire region follows the law of *Muhammad*. And from here, from *Yarkand*, the start of the expedition into *Cathay* by *Caravan* begins, at least for those who know for certain they will be admitted into the Kingdom. Almost the entire trade consists of fragments of precious Jasper <note>term: *Jaspidis* - here referring to Jade</note>, which are highly valued in *Cathay*, that is, *China*. It is of two kinds: the first is drawn out from the river *Khotan* not far from the royal city by fishermen, like thick ferns; the other, dug out from the mountains, is split into stone slabs nearly two cubits long. This Mountain *Cansangui* is distant from the royal city 
<margin>
The Stone Mountain.
</margin>
by about twenty days, and is called the *Stone Mountain*, commonly noted on geographical maps. From here, therefore, after a long delay, the journey...
